\title{Closure Conversion: Definitions}
\author{}
\date{}

\usepackage[colorlinks=true,linkcolor=blue,urlcolor=blue,bookmarksnumbered=true]{hyperref}

\begin{document}
\maketitle
\tableofcontents

\section*{About this document}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{About this document}

This is a summary, for review, of the definitions in the Agda development
of the closure-conversion compiler in \texttt{agda}: the languages in
\texttt{agda/Language} (the typed ones in \texttt{agda/Language/Typed}), the
passes in \texttt{agda/Compiler}, and the semantics of each style in
\texttt{agda/Semantics} (of the typed languages in its \texttt{Typed}
subdirectories). Part~\ref{part:typed} presents the typed pipeline, the main
one: the types, the typed languages, cast insertion and the typed passes, the
semantics of the typed languages in each style with the relations of each
style's proofs, and the theorems; it has its own notes on where it doesn't
match the Agda literally, at the end of its sections.
Part~\ref{part:untyped} presents the untyped pipeline that preceded it,
preserved in \texttt{agda/Untyped}, with the definitions both share (ISWIM,
simplify, X86, and their semantics); the contents list and the notes below
are those of Part~\ref{part:untyped}.
It uses the notation of \texttt{talks/ITRS-2020} and named variables
instead of de Bruijn indices.
The public theorem statements are in \texttt{agda/Theorems}; they and the
definitions they use (everything outside \texttt{agda/proof}) are the
audit surface, and the proofs are in \texttt{agda/proof}, which mirrors
the paths of the surface, with the correctness proofs of the passes in
\texttt{agda/proof/Correctness} and adequacy in
\texttt{agda/proof/Adequacy} (\texttt{agda/README.md}).
The language-independent infrastructure is in \texttt{agda/Framework}:
the graph model's values and operators in \texttt{Framework/GraphModel},
the syntax in \texttt{Framework/abt} and \texttt{Framework/Syntax},
and the small-step and clocked frameworks, parameterized over the
observations, in \texttt{Framework/Smallstep.agda} and
\texttt{Framework/Clocked.agda}; their lemmas (continuity, the generic
denotational lemmas, the syntax lemmas, forward simulations) are under
\texttt{agda/proof/Framework}. The meaning of the primitives is in
\texttt{Semantics/Graph/Prim.agda}.

\paragraph{Contents.}
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{The compiler:}
  \begin{itemize}
  \item the syntax of ISWIM and Clos2--Clos5, including Clos5 programs,
    and the primitives $\key{sub}$ and $\key{eq}$;
  \item enclose, optimize, concretize, delay (with the let), and globalize;
  \item remove complex operands (rco) into monadic Clos5, $\pass{compile\text{-}mon}$,
    and well-scoped Clos5 programs ($\mathit{Scoped}_5$);
  \item the imperative language Imp, lift locals into it, and
    $\pass{compile\text{-}imp}$;
  \item the control-flow-graph language CFG, explicate control into it, and
    $\pass{compile\text{-}cfg}$;
  \item x86 with variables over abstract values (X86), select
    instructions into it, and $\pass{compile\text{-}x86}$.
  \end{itemize}
\item \textbf{Denotational: graph model:}
  \begin{itemize}
  \item the value domain and environments, with $\rho_0(x) = \{\omega\}$;
  \item the operators $\star$, $\Lambda$, $\key{pair}$/$\key{car}$/$\key{cdr}$,
    strict $\mathcal{T}$, $\key{proj}$, $\key{inl}$/$\key{inr}$/$\mathcal{C}$,
    $\key{guard}$, $\key{prim}_p$, and $\odot$;
  \item consistency ($\sim$, $\approx$, $\consis$), the binders $\Lambda$ and
    $\mathcal{C}$ binding only consistent sets, the preservation of a relation
    by a semantics, and continuity;
  \item the semantics: the clauses all languages share, then each language's own
    clauses, including Clos5's table-indexed semantics, $\key{code}$, and $\key{table}$;
  \item what the semantics of Imp and CFG share: stores, $\mathit{read}_n$,
    $\mathit{checked}_n$, atoms $\mathcal{A}$, simple expressions $\mathcal{S}$,
    $\key{frame}$, and $\key{table}$;
  \item the continuation-passing semantics of Imp (the main one), and the direct
    one, with ${\downarrow}$, $\key{let}_s$, and $\key{case}_s$;
  \item the semantics of CFG, with $\key{blocks}$ and $\mathit{run}$;
  \item the semantics of X86: the frame, registers, and flag, strict
    writes ($\key{put}$, $\key{flag}$), the instructions in
    continuation-passing style, $\key{callq}$, $\key{code}$, and $\key{table}$;
  \item the enclose and optimize equalities;
  \item for concretize, the $\mathit{Ref}$ semantics and $\sim_\varphi$;
  \item for delay, the let equation, the element-wise relation $\sqsubseteq$
    for preservation, and $\mathit{depth}$, $\mathcal{R}$, and $\rhd$
    for reflection;
  \item for globalize, $\mathit{Extends}$, $\mathit{RB}_n$, and $\mathit{Agree}_n$;
  \item for rco, the let laws, strict operators, the let chain, and the
    rco equation;
  \item for lift locals, $\mathit{Agree}$ and $\mathit{Scope}$, and, against the
    continuation-passing semantics, the enclosing lets $K$ and the let and
    case laws; the lemma against the direct semantics;
  \item for explicate control, $\preceq$ on block lists, $\mathit{Stable}$,
    $\key{assignK}$, the continuation-passing lemma, and the theorem; the
    theorem against the direct semantics;
  \item for select instructions, $\mathit{Tagged}$, $\mathit{Inv}$,
    $\mathit{Fits}$, and $\key{after}$, the lemmas for simple expressions,
    case, tails, monotone CFG tails, and the start of a definition, and the
    theorem for definitions of at least 2 slots;
  \item the end-to-end theorems, for $\pass{compile}$, $\pass{compile\text{-}mon}$,
    $\pass{compile\text{-}imp}$, $\pass{compile\text{-}cfg}$, and $\pass{compile\text{-}x86}$.
  \end{itemize}
\item \textbf{CEK machine + simulation:}
  \begin{itemize}
  \item observations, machines as step functions, termination, divergence,
    and determinism;
  \item the machines of ISWIM, Clos2--Clos5, Imp, and CFG: values, continuation frames,
    and transitions; the evaluation of simple expressions ($\mathit{first}$,
    $\mathit{resume}$) that Imp and CFG share;
  \item the X86 machine: states with a call stack, flag, frame, and
    registers, and its transitions, with $\key{callq}$, $\key{retq}$, and jumps;
  \item forward simulations, $\mathit{Equivalent}$, and the theorem that a
    forward simulation gives $\mathit{Equivalent}$;
  \item for each pass, the relations $\approx$, $\approx_k$, and $\sim$ on
    values, continuations, and states, and its correctness theorem;
  \item for rco, $\preceq$ on environments, the pending let chain
    $\mathit{Pend}$, and its frames;
  \item for lift locals, frames related to environments ($\approx^n_\varphi$), and
    continuations given such a frame (head, weakened, and calls);
  \item for explicate control, the modes, $\preceq$, the relations $\approx$,
    $\mathit{Ctl}$, and $\mathit{Br}$ on continuations, the states indexed by
    $\mathit{depth}$, and the join lemma;
  \item for select instructions, $\approx$ between continuations and call
    stacks, $\mathit{Dest}$, the synchronisation points $\sim$, and the
    scripts $\mathit{Fate}$;
  \item the end-to-end theorems, for $\pass{compile}$, $\pass{compile\text{-}mon}$,
    $\pass{compile\text{-}imp}$, $\pass{compile\text{-}cfg}$, and $\pass{compile\text{-}x86}$;
  \item backward simulations, and the direct backward simulation for delay.
  \end{itemize}
\item \textbf{Clocked interpreter + value relations:}
  \begin{itemize}
  \item results, clocked programs, termination, divergence, and $\mathit{Equivalent}$;
  \item $\mathit{RelResult}$, and the theorem that related results for every
    clock give $\mathit{Equivalent}$;
  \item the clocked interpreters of ISWIM, Clos2--Clos5, Imp, CFG, and X86, with
    $\key{eval\text{-}simple}$, $\key{inj?}$, and $\key{set}$ shared by Imp and CFG;
  \item for each pass, the lemma relating the interpreters clock for clock,
    and its correctness theorem; for rco, with equal results, and the let chain;
    for lift locals, with $\mathit{Out}$; for explicate control, with $\sqsubseteq$,
    $\mathit{Means}$, and $\mathit{Ok}$; for select instructions, with equal results;
  \item the end-to-end theorems, for $\pass{compile}$, $\pass{compile\text{-}mon}$,
    $\pass{compile\text{-}imp}$, $\pass{compile\text{-}cfg}$, and $\pass{compile\text{-}x86}$.
  \end{itemize}
\item \textbf{Reduction semantics + simulation:}
  \begin{itemize}
  \item reductions as machines ($\mathit{Red}$, $\mathit{toNext}$), and the
    reduction semantics of ISWIM and Clos2--Clos5 by substitution, with
    evaluation contexts; Imp, CFG, and X86 keep their machines;
  \item simulations between reductions: answering a stop by a run to a final
    state, $\mathit{Ans}$, congruence, the theorem that a simulation gives
    $\mathit{Equivalent}$, and closure under substitution;
  \item for each pass, the relation on terms, its fundamental lemma, and its
    correctness theorem; for rco, the let chain related for every value; for
    lift locals, terms in a context of lets related to Imp states;
  \item the end-to-end theorems, for $\pass{compile}$, $\pass{compile\text{-}mon}$,
    $\pass{compile\text{-}imp}$, $\pass{compile\text{-}cfg}$, and $\pass{compile\text{-}x86}$.
  \end{itemize}
\end{enumerate}

\paragraph{Where it doesn't match the Agda literally.}
Using names instead of de Bruijn indices changes a few definitions:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Enclose} lists $\FV(\lam{y} N)$ as the closure's free variables.
  The Agda lists the first $m$ surrounding variables, enough to cover every
  variable the body uses.
\item \textbf{Closure free variables} are variables in Clos2 and
  references in Clos3, which is all the passes produce. The Agda allows
  arbitrary terms there.
\item \textbf{Optimize} has no renaming parameter. It keeps $x_i$ when
  $x_i \in \FV(N)$.
\item \textbf{Concretize}'s map has no fallback for variables past the free
  variables. Closed code can't refer to them.
\item \textbf{Tuple indices} run from $1$ to $n$.
\item \textbf{Global definitions} are listed oldest first, so $\funref{k}$ names $d_k$.
\item \textbf{Clos5 definitions} are closed by the grammar. The Agda's
  syntax allows any definition; $\mathit{Scoped}_5$ is the Agda's
  \texttt{Scoped} (\texttt{Compiler/PipelineScope.agda}).
\item \textbf{Fresh variables} of rco are de Bruijn indices in the Agda:
  each let shifts the operands after it, and the atoms under the chain.
\item \textbf{Lift locals} has no map $\varphi$ in the Agda: variable $x$
  at depth $d$ is slot $d - (x + 1)$, its de Bruijn level, and slot $n$ when
  $x \geq d$. The Agda defines the pass on derivations that its input is
  in monadic normal form (\texttt{Mon}).
\item \textbf{Lift locals}' relations ($\mathit{Agree}$, $\approx^n_\varphi$,
  $\mathit{Out}$) relate the frame to the environment through $\varphi$.
  In the Agda, at depth $d$ (the length of the environment $\rho$ in the
  operational proofs) variable $x$ is slot $d - (x + 1)$, so the frame
  is $\rho$ reversed followed by the slots above it ($\rho \mathbin{{}^{r}{+}\!\!{+}} \tau$),
  and $\mathit{Scope}$ asks $\rho(x) \simeq \{\omega\}$ for $x \geq d$.
\item \textbf{Explicate control} threads the list of blocks explicitly
  in the Agda, newest first: block $t_l$ is the one with $l$ blocks after
  it in the list, and is looked up with the blocks older than it
  (\texttt{block}). So a list that extends $\vec{t}$ with newer blocks
  ($\vec{t} \preceq \vec{t}'$, $\vec{t} \sqsubseteq \vec{t}'$) has $\vec{t}$
  as a suffix in the Agda, not a prefix.
\item \textbf{Consistency} of a set, $\consis(D)$, is the Agda's \texttt{Con};
  of an environment or a store, $\consis(\rho)$, is \texttt{Con-env}; and
  $D \approx E$ on sets is \texttt{Every \_\char`~\_ D E}.
\item \begin{sloppypar}\textbf{The direct and continuation-passing semantics of Imp} are
  \texttt{ImpDirect.agda} (an experiment, under
  \texttt{proof/Semantics/Graph}) and \texttt{ImpK.agda} (under
  \texttt{Semantics/Graph}); what they share with CFG is in
  \texttt{Imp.agda}.\end{sloppypar}
\item \begin{sloppypar}\textbf{Compiler correctness} is stated with $\obseq$.
  The Agda proves its two halves as separate theorems,
  \texttt{compile-correct-const} and \texttt{compile-correct-nonempty},
  and likewise for $\pass{compile\text{-}imp}$ and $\pass{compile\text{-}cfg}$:
  \texttt{compile-imp-correct-const}, \texttt{compile-imp-correct-nonempty},
  \texttt{compile-cfg-correct-const},
  \texttt{compile-cfg-correct-nonempty}, \texttt{compile-x86-correct-const},
  and \texttt{compile-x86-correct-nonempty}.\end{sloppypar}
\item \textbf{The X86 semantics}' strict write of a register or the flag,
  $\key{guard}(D; E)$, is the Agda's \texttt{strict D E}; the flag is
  \texttt{left}~$\emptytup$ or \texttt{right}~$\emptytup$. Its blocks and
  table are listed newest first, as in CFG.
\item \begin{sloppypar}\textbf{Select instructions}' precondition is \texttt{Defs}, and
  that $\pass{compile\text{-}cfg}$ meets it is \texttt{defs-2}; the
  lemmas are \texttt{sel}, \texttt{case-core}, \texttt{tail},
  \texttt{blocks-}$\simeq$, \texttt{Mono.tail-mono}, \texttt{frame-}$\simeq$,
  and \texttt{start-}$\simeq$ (\texttt{proof/Correctness/Graph/SelectCorrect.agda}).
  $B \mathrel{\mathit{BR}} B'$ is \texttt{BR B B'}.\end{sloppypar}
\end{itemize}
In the simulation proof:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Machine environments} are finite maps from variables.
  The Agda uses lists indexed by de Bruijn indices, and a Clos2 closure
  holds the values of its free variables last first.
\item \textbf{Values collected} in $\key{tupK}$ and $\key{closK}$ frames are
  listed in order. The Agda lists them in reverse.
\item \textbf{Primitives} test their operands through their observations
  ($\delta^?$ on $\obs(v)$ and $\obs(w)$), which are integers
  exactly when the values are.
\item \textbf{Projections} $\prj{i} M$ carry the arity $n$ of the tuple,
  as \texttt{get} does in the Agda ($i : \mathtt{Fin}\;n$).
\item \textbf{Enclose}'s $\approx_X$ needs no bound on the Clos2 environment.
  The Agda's \texttt{Agree} also asks that the Clos2 environment covers
  the variables the closure captures.
\item \textbf{Optimize}'s relations need no renaming. The Agda relates
  variable $x$ to $r(x)$ for the renaming $r$ that optimize carries, and
  the values collected for a closure through the renumbering of positions
  that \texttt{prune} computes. $\mathit{Scoped}$ is \texttt{WS}, and
  $\mathit{WS}(M)$ is \texttt{WS 0 M}.
\item \textbf{Closed} ISWIM terms, $\FV(M) = \emptyset$, are the Agda's
  \texttt{Closed}.
\item \textbf{Concretize}'s $\approx_\varphi$ ranges over the domain of
  $\varphi$. The Agda's $\varphi$ is total, mapping a variable past a
  closure's free variables to an unbound one, so its theorem has no
  precondition.
\item \textbf{Delay}'s $\approx^{?}$ on environments skips the fresh variables $f$.
  The Agda relates environments through a renaming that skips the
  let-bound variables.
\item \textbf{Global definitions} in the Agda's Clos5 machine and globalize
  relation are listed newest first.
\item \textbf{RCO}'s $\rho_1 \preceq \rho_2$ is the Agda's \texttt{Ren}: the
  source term $M$ is related to \texttt{rename r (rco M)} for a renaming
  \texttt{r} with $\rho_2(r(x)) = \rho_1(x)$. The Agda's frames are indexed
  by the kind of construct (\texttt{Frame}, \texttt{Kind}), and its
  $\mathit{Pend}$ takes the values and operands as lists.
\item \textbf{Lift locals}' relations assume that the variables bound in a
  body are distinct from each other and from its parameters, so extending
  $\rho$ never shadows. The Agda's frame $\rho \mathbin{{}^{r}{+}\!\!{+}} \tau$ keeps a
  shadowed value in its slot, so it needs no such assumption.
  $\vdash_n$ and $\vdash^{\mathsf{h}}_n$ are \texttt{Kont} and \texttt{KontH},
  and $\approx^n_\varphi$ is \texttt{Fr}.
\item \textbf{The frames of simple expressions} $F$ in the Imp and CFG
  machines are wrapped in a continuation by \texttt{frm}: $F \cdot \kappa$ is
  \texttt{frm F $\kappa$}.
\item \begin{sloppypar}\textbf{Explicate}'s Imp states $\evi{a}{\sigma}{\kappa}$, for an
  atom $a$, are the Agda's \texttt{eval (simple (atm a)) $\varphi$ $\kappa$}.
  The machines and the simulation are in \texttt{Semantics/CEK/CFG.agda} and
  \texttt{proof/Correctness/CEK/ExplicateSim.agda}.\end{sloppypar}
\item \textbf{The X86 machine}'s state $\xrun\;\vec{\imath}\;j\;\vec{\beta}\;\Sigma\;f\;\sigma\;\rho$
  is the Agda's \texttt{run is j bs} $\sigma$ \texttt{f} $\varphi$ $\rho$, where
  $\sigma$ is the call stack and $\varphi$ the frame.
\item \textbf{Select instructions}' $\kappa \approx \Sigma$ is the Agda's
  \texttt{K \char`\~{}} $\sigma$, $s_1 \sim s_2$ is \texttt{Match} (with
  \texttt{exec\char`\~}, \texttt{ret\char`\~}, and \texttt{now}), and the
  scripts' cases are \texttt{stop}, \texttt{lag}, and \texttt{go}
  (\texttt{proof/Correctness/CEK/SelectSim.agda}). Lemmas~\ref{lem:to-dest},
  \ref{lem:sel-simple-sim}, and~\ref{lem:fate} are \texttt{to-dest},
  \texttt{simple}, and \texttt{fate}.
\end{itemize}
In the interpreter proof:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Environments, values, and relations} are those of the
  simulation proof, so the items above about them apply here too.
\item \textbf{Values collected} by $\key{evals}$ are listed in order. The
  Agda's \texttt{eval-args} conses them onto an accumulator, last first,
  and a tuple reverses them.
\item \textbf{Optimize}'s lemma for the free variables of a closure is
  stated with $\mathit{Built}$. The Agda's \texttt{prune}$\approx$ relates the
  values collected so far through the renumbering of positions that
  \texttt{prune} computes (\texttt{Collected}).
\item \textbf{Delay}'s lemma relates $M$ to $\pass{delay}(M)$. The Agda
  relates $M$ to \texttt{rename r (delay M)} for a renaming \texttt{r}
  of the environments, since the operand of an application is evaluated
  under the let-bound variable.
\item \textbf{The Clos5 interpreter}'s table is listed newest first in the
  Agda, which looks up definition $j$ in its reverse (\texttt{global}).
\item \textbf{Clocked programs} are the Agda's \texttt{Semantics}, and
  $\outc$ is \texttt{outcome}, which returns a \texttt{Maybe Obs}.
\item \textbf{The Imp interpreter} collects the values of the atoms of a
  tuple onto an accumulator, last first, as Clos5's does.
\item \textbf{The CFG interpreter}'s $\key{tail}$ and $\key{jump}$ are
  structurally recursive on the clock, then the list of blocks, then the
  tail: a jump walks the list, newest first, to its block.
\item \textbf{The X86 interpreter}'s registers are a record
  (\texttt{Regs}) of \texttt{Maybe}s for $\rax$, $\rdi$, $\rsi$, and the
  flag; its $\key{cmpq}$ applies \texttt{bool?} to
  $\delta^?(\key{eq}, \obs(v), \obs(w))$, which is $\key{cmp}(v, w)$.
  It is structurally recursive on the clock, the list of blocks, and the
  instructions of a block.
\item \textbf{Select instructions}' lemmas are \texttt{simple-ok},
  \texttt{tail-ok}, and \texttt{jump-ok}, and calls agree by
  \texttt{calls}$\equiv$ and \texttt{call-pw}
  (\texttt{proof/Correctness/Interp/SelectFuel.agda}).
\end{itemize}

In the reduction proof:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Substitution} is on de Bruijn terms in the Agda
  (\texttt{Framework/Syntax/Subst.agda}), and leaves the cleared code of a
  closure untouched. The reduction semantics are in
  \texttt{Semantics/Reduction}, written as functions with congruence rules
  (\texttt{step-in}) rather than with evaluation contexts.
\item \textbf{Values} are a predicate on terms (\texttt{Value}), and a
  simulation answers with a target value (\texttt{Ans}, \texttt{val}).
\item \textbf{Enclose}'s closure clause relates the variables that the
  body uses (\texttt{Uses}) and keeps the target closed (\texttt{WS 0}).
\item \textbf{The preconditions} $\FV(M) = \emptyset$ of optimize and
  concretize are the Agda's \texttt{WS 0 M}.
\item \textbf{RCO}'s contexts $h$ are frames of the Clos5 reduction
  (\texttt{Frame}), and the operands of a construct are reduced left to right
  (\texttt{LR}); the let chains are those of \texttt{lift}.
\item \textbf{Lift locals}' frame is $\rho$ reversed followed by the slots
  above it, as in the CEK proof (\texttt{Fr}), with $\sigma$ related to
  $\rho$ (\texttt{Ag}) and the context to the continuation (\texttt{Kont}).
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Building.} \texttt{latexmk -pdf definitions.tex}

%===============================================================================
\part{The Typed Pipeline}\label{part:typed}

The compiler of the main development:
\[
\begin{array}{l}
\mathrm{ISWIM} \xrightarrow{\pass{simplify}} \mathrm{ISWIM} \xrightarrow{\pass{cast}} \Tl{ISWIM}
  \xrightarrow{\pass{enclose}} \Tl{Clos2} \xrightarrow{\pass{optimize}} \Tl{Clos2}
  \xrightarrow{\pass{concretize}} \Tl{Clos3} \xrightarrow{\pass{delay}} \Tl{Clos4} \\
\qquad \xrightarrow{\pass{globalize}} \Tl{Clos5} \xrightarrow{\pass{rco}} \Tl{Mon}
  \xrightarrow{\pass{lift}} \Tl{Imp} \xrightarrow{\pass{explicate}} \Tl{CFG}
  \xrightarrow{\pass{select}} \mathrm{X86}
\end{array}
\]
The source ISWIM, simplify, the target X86 and the semantics of ISWIM and X86 in each style are
those of Part~\ref{part:untyped}, which both pipelines share.


%===============================================================================
\section{Types and Typed Languages}\label{sec:t-langs}

The typed pipeline makes the dynamically typed source statically typed by
cast insertion, after \emph{Essentials of Compilation}, ch.~9, and keeps
every intermediate language intrinsically typed, so that a term that
misuses a value (applies a tuple, reads a slot not yet assigned, jumps to
a missing block) cannot be written. ISWIM itself, X86, and simplify are
those of the untyped pipeline (Part~II); the typed languages are in
\texttt{agda/Language/Typed}. In each language, a term is indexed by its
type; a variable, slot, definition, or label is a typed index, so every
term is well scoped by construction.

\subsection{Types}

A value of type $\TAny$ is a value of some flat type $G$, tagged with it.
The flat types mirror the values of the untyped languages: a constant of a
base type $B$, a tuple of $n$ values of type $\TAny$, a sum ($\key{inl}$ or
$\key{inr}$ of a value of type $\TAny$), and a function from $\TAny$ to
$\TAny$ (a closure in $\Tl{Clos2}$ and $\Tl{Clos3}$, a packed pair of code and
environment from $\Tl{Clos4}$ on). The type $\Tcode{n}$, from $\Tl{Clos4}$ on, is
that of closed code that takes an environment, a tuple of $n$ values, and
an argument. $\Tinject{G}$ turns a value of type $\Tflat{G}$ into one of
type $\TAny$, and $\Tproject{t}$ turns it back; $\key{project}$ is the only
operation that can fail: it stops with a trapped error, $\error$, when the
tag is not $\Tflatof{t}$. The tags $t$ are the flat types that
$\key{project}$ can test, those that cast insertion projects to; each is
tested by an existing X86 instruction that errs on any other value
(Section~\ref{sec:t-passes}). The flat type of a primitive's result is
$\Tout{p}$.
\begin{syntax}
  & B &::=& \key{Nat} \mid \key{Int} \mid \key{Bool} \\
  & G, H &::=& B \mid \Ttup{n} \mid \Tsum \mid \Tfun \\
  & A &::=& \TAny \mid \Tflat{G} \mid \Tcode{n} \\
  & t &::=& \Tfun \mid \Tsum \mid \Tint \mid \Tttup{n}{i}
    \qquad (1 \leq i \leq n) \\
  & \Gamma &::=& x_1{:}A_1, \ldots, x_k{:}A_k
\end{syntax}
\[
  \Tflatof{\Tfun} = \Tfun \quad
  \Tflatof{\Tsum} = \Tsum \quad
  \Tflatof{\Tint} = \key{Int} \quad
  \Tflatof{\Tttup{n}{i}} = \Ttup{n}
\]
\[
  \Tout{\key{sub}} = \key{Int} \quad
  \Tout{\key{eq}} = \Tsum
\]
A tuple tag $\Tttup{n}{i}$ is for a tuple of $n \geq 1$ components
(cast insertion projects to it only for a $\prj{i}$, which needs at least
one), and names the component $i$ that the projection reads, which is
where X86 tests the tuple. A value of type $\TAny$ carries its flat type,
and $\key{project}$ compares it with $\Tflatof{t}$, so flat types have
decidable equality.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{ISWIM}$}{ISWIM-T}}

ISWIM with explicit casts, the output of cast insertion. Every variable has
type $\TAny$ (functions, $\key{let}$ and $\key{case}$ bind values of type
$\TAny$); the operations take operands of the flat type they need, and
$\key{inject}$ is the only way into $\TAny$.
\begin{syntax}
  & L, M, N &::=& x \mid \lam{x} N \mid L\;M \mid c
    \mid \tuple{M_1,\ldots,M_n} \mid \prj{i} M
    \mid \inl M \mid \inr M \\
  &&& \mid \Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N} \mid \prim{p}{L}{M}
    \mid \Let x = M \In N
    \mid \Tinject{G} M \mid \Tproject{t} M
\end{syntax}
\[
\begin{array}{r@{\;:\;}l@{\qquad}l}
  x & \TAny & \\
  \lam{x} N & \Tflat{\Tfun} & N : \TAny \\
  L\;M & \TAny & L : \Tflat{\Tfun},\ M : \TAny \\
  c & \Tflat{B} & c \text{ a constant of base type } B \\
  \tuple{M_1,\ldots,M_n} & \Tflat{\Ttup{n}} & M_1, \ldots, M_n : \TAny \\
  \prj{i} M & \TAny & M : \Tflat{\Ttup{n}},\ 1 \leq i \leq n \\
  \inl M,\ \inr M & \Tflat{\Tsum} & M : \TAny \\
  \Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N} & \TAny & L : \Tflat{\Tsum},\ M, N : \TAny \\
  \prim{p}{L}{M} & \Tflat{\Tout{p}} & L, M : \Tflat{\key{Int}} \\
  \Let x = M \In N & \TAny & M, N : \TAny \\
  \Tinject{G} M & \TAny & M : \Tflat{G} \\
  \Tproject{t} M & \Tflat{\Tflatof{t}} & M : \TAny
\end{array}
\]

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos2}$}{Clos2-T}}

As $\Tl{ISWIM}$, but each $\lambda$ is a closure, as in Clos2: its code $N$ is
closed, with the parameter $y$ and the names $x_1 \ldots x_n$ bound to the
values of $M_1 \ldots M_n$, all of type $\TAny$.
\begin{syntax}
  & L, M, N &::=& x \mid \clos{M_1\ldots M_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N
    \mid L\;M \mid c \mid \cdots \mid \Tinject{G} M \mid \Tproject{t} M
\end{syntax}
\[
  \clos{M_1\ldots M_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N : \Tflat{\Tfun}
  \qquad M_1, \ldots, M_n : \TAny,\quad
    x_1{:}\TAny, \ldots, x_n{:}\TAny, y{:}\TAny \vdash N : \TAny
\]

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos3}$}{Clos3-T}}

Each closure stores its free variables in one tuple $e$, from which its
code reads them with projections. So variables now have types other than
$\TAny$, and a term is typed in a context $\Gamma$, $\Gamma \vdash M : A$.
The closure itself has type $\Tflat{\Tfun}$: the length $n$ of its
environment is hidden in its value.
\begin{syntax}
  & L, M, N &::=& x \mid \clos{M_1\ldots M_n}{e\, y} N
    \mid L\;M \mid c \mid \cdots \mid \Tinject{G} M \mid \Tproject{t} M
\end{syntax}
\[
  \frac{x{:}A \in \Gamma}{\Gamma \vdash x : A}
  \qquad
  \frac{\Gamma \vdash M_1, \ldots, M_n : \TAny \qquad
        y{:}\TAny,\, e{:}\Tflat{\Ttup{n}} \vdash N : \TAny}
       {\Gamma \vdash \clos{M_1\ldots M_n}{e\, y} N : \Tflat{\Tfun}}
\]
The other constructs are typed as in $\Tl{ISWIM}$; $\key{let}$ and
$\key{case}$ still bind variables of type $\TAny$.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos4}$}{Clos4-T}}

A closure is a pair of closed code and the tuple of its free variables,
packed so that its type $\Tflat{\Tfun}$ hides the length $n$ of the tuple:
$\Tflat{\Tfun}$ is the existential $\exists n.\, \Tcode{n} \times
\Tflat{\Ttup{n}}$ of typed closure conversion (Minamide, Morrisett and
Harper). $\key{pack}$ builds a closure from code and an environment of the
same length; $\key{callc}$ opens a closure and calls its code with its
environment and the argument. $\key{callc}$ is the only elimination of the
existential and is parametric in $n$, so it needs no check and compiles to
one sequence of instructions for every length. There are no pairs,
$\key{fst}$ or $\key{snd}$, as there are in Clos4.
\begin{syntax}
  & L, M, N &::=& x \mid \Tcodetm{e}{y}{N} \mid \Tpack{L}{M} \mid \Tcallc{L}{M}
    \mid c \mid \tuple{M_1,\ldots,M_n} \mid \prj{i} M \mid \inl M \mid \inr M \\
  &&& \mid \Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N} \mid \prim{p}{L}{M}
    \mid \Let x = M \In N \mid \Tinject{G} M \mid \Tproject{t} M
\end{syntax}
\[
  \frac{y{:}\TAny,\, e{:}\Tflat{\Ttup{n}} \vdash N : \TAny}
       {\Gamma \vdash \Tcodetm{e}{y}{N} : \Tcode{n}}
  \qquad
  \frac{\Gamma \vdash L : \Tcode{n} \quad \Gamma \vdash M : \Tflat{\Ttup{n}}}
       {\Gamma \vdash \Tpack{L}{M} : \Tflat{\Tfun}}
  \qquad
  \frac{\Gamma \vdash L : \Tflat{\Tfun} \quad \Gamma \vdash M : \TAny}
       {\Gamma \vdash \Tcallc{L}{M} : \TAny}
\]

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos5}$}{Clos5-T}}

As $\Tl{Clos4}$, but the code of every function is a global definition. A
program has a table $\Sigma = n_0, \ldots, n_{m-1}$, the lengths of the
environments of its definitions $d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1}$, and a term is
typed by the table as well as by its context, $\Sigma; \Gamma \vdash M :
A$: $\funref{k}$ is the code of $d_k$, of type $\Tcode{n_k}$, so a reference
to a missing definition cannot be written. Every definition is typed over
the whole table. Unlike $\Tl{Clos4}$, $\key{let}$ binds a value of any type,
for remove complex operands, which binds operands of any type (code,
environments, \ldots).
\begin{syntax}
  & L, M, N &::=& x \mid \funref{k} \mid \Tpack{L}{M} \mid \Tcallc{L}{M}
    \mid c \mid \tuple{M_1,\ldots,M_n} \mid \prj{i} M \mid \inl M \mid \inr M \\
  &&& \mid \Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N} \mid \prim{p}{L}{M}
    \mid \Let x = M \In N \mid \Tinject{G} M \mid \Tproject{t} M \\[1ex]
  & d &::=& \Tcodetm{e}{y}{N} \\
  & P &::=& (d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)
\end{syntax}
\[
  \frac{k < m}{\Sigma; \Gamma \vdash \funref{k} : \Tcode{n_k}}
  \qquad
  \frac{\Sigma; \Gamma \vdash M : A \quad \Sigma; \Gamma, x{:}A \vdash N : B}
       {\Sigma; \Gamma \vdash \Let x = M \In N : B}
\]
\[
  \frac{\Sigma; y{:}\TAny, e{:}\Tflat{\Ttup{n_k}} \vdash N_k : \TAny \ (d_k = \Tcodetm{e}{y}{N_k})
        \quad \Sigma; \emptyset \vdash M : \TAny}
       {\vdash (d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)}
\]

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Mon}$}{Mon-T}}

The monadic normal form of $\Tl{Clos5}$. Untyped monadic Clos5 is a predicate
on Clos5 terms; $\Tl{Mon}$ is its own syntax, so monadic form needs no proof,
and its meaning is that of its embedding into $\Tl{Clos5}$. An atom is a
variable, a constant or a reference to a definition; the operands of every
operation, and the scrutinee of $\key{case}$, are atoms, while the
right-hand side and body of $\key{let}$ (at any type, as in $\Tl{Clos5}$) and
the branches of $\key{case}$ are expressions. Typing is as in $\Tl{Clos5}$.
\begin{syntax}
  & a &::=& x \mid c \mid \funref{k} \\
  & M, N &::=& a \mid \Tpack{a_1}{a_2} \mid \Tcallc{a_1}{a_2}
    \mid \tuple{a_1,\ldots,a_n} \mid \prj{i} a \mid \inl a \mid \inr a
    \mid \prim{p}{a_1}{a_2} \\
  &&& \mid \Tinject{G} a \mid \Tproject{t} a
    \mid \Case{a}{x}{M}{y}{N} \mid \Let x = M \In N \\[1ex]
  & d &::=& \Tcodetm{e}{y}{N} \\
  & P &::=& (d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)
\end{syntax}

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Imp}$}{Imp-T}}

Imp, typed. A body runs in a frame of slots, and the frame is typed by a
context $\Delta = A_0, \ldots, A_{m-1}$, the types of the slots assigned so
far, so that slot $s < m$ holds a value of type $A_s$. A slot is a typed
index into $\Delta$, so a slot out of the frame, or not yet assigned, cannot
be read. Assignment and $\key{case}$ always write the next slot, $|\Delta|$,
which the rest of the body sees. The size of a frame is not part of a body:
it is the deepest nesting of slots, which select instructions computes. The
simple expressions $r$ are shared with $\Tl{CFG}$; they and the atoms are those
of $\Tl{Mon}$, over slots instead of variables.
\begin{syntax}
  & a &::=& s \mid c \mid \funref{k} \\
  & r &::=& a \mid \Tpack{a_1}{a_2} \mid \Tcallc{a_1}{a_2}
    \mid \tuple{a_1,\ldots,a_n} \mid \prj{i} a \mid \inl a \mid \inr a
    \mid \prim{p}{a_1}{a_2} \mid \Tinject{G} a \mid \Tproject{t} a \\
  & e &::=& r \mid \assign{s}{e_1}{e_2} \mid \Casei{a}{s}{e_1}{e_2} \\[1ex]
  & P &::=& (e_0, \ldots, e_{m-1};\; e)
\end{syntax}
\[
  \frac{\Sigma; \Delta \vdash e_1 : A \quad \Sigma; \Delta, A \vdash e_2 : B}
       {\Sigma; \Delta \vdash \assign{|\Delta|}{e_1}{e_2} : B}
  \qquad
  \frac{\Sigma; \Delta \vdash a : \Tflat{\Tsum} \quad
        \Sigma; \Delta, \TAny \vdash e_1, e_2 : \TAny}
       {\Sigma; \Delta \vdash \Casei{a}{|\Delta|}{e_1}{e_2} : \TAny}
\]
\[
  \Sigma; \Delta \vdash s : A_s
  \qquad
  \Sigma; \Tflat{\Ttup{n_k}}, \TAny \vdash e_k : \TAny
  \qquad
  \Sigma; \emptyset \vdash e : \TAny
\]
A definition starts with its environment in slot $0$ and its argument in
slot $1$; $\key{main}$ with no slot.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{CFG}$}{CFG-T}}

CFG, typed. A block is a tail typed by the frame $\Delta$ it starts in (the
types of the slots it may read) and by the list $\vec{\Delta} = \Delta_0,
\ldots, \Delta_{j-1}$ of the frames of the blocks it may jump to,
$\Sigma; \vec{\Delta}; \Delta \vdash t$. A label $l$ is a typed index into
$\vec{\Delta}$, so a jump to a missing block, or to a block that reads a
slot not assigned, cannot be written. Block $t_l$ is typed over the blocks
older than it, so control flow is acyclic, as in CFG; the start may jump
to any block. An assignment, and the $\key{case}$ that assigns the payload,
write slot $s = |\Delta'|$ for a prefix $\Delta'$ of $\Delta$: the slots
from $s$ on are forgotten (they belong to a finished scope) and slot $s$ is
assigned anew.
\begin{syntax}
  & t &::=& \Treturn{r} \mid \assign{s}{r}{t} \mid \Tgoto{l}
    \mid \Tcase{a}{s}{l_1}{l_2} \\[1ex]
  & b &::=& (t_0, \ldots, t_{j-1};\; t) \\
  & P &::=& (b_0, \ldots, b_{m-1};\; b)
\end{syntax}
\[
  \frac{\Sigma; \Delta \vdash r : \TAny}{\Sigma; \vec{\Delta}; \Delta \vdash \Treturn{r}}
  \qquad
  \frac{\Delta' \sqsubseteq \Delta \quad \Sigma; \Delta \vdash r : A \quad
        \Sigma; \vec{\Delta}; \Delta', A \vdash t}
       {\Sigma; \vec{\Delta}; \Delta \vdash \assign{|\Delta'|}{r}{t}}
  \qquad
  \frac{\Delta_l = \Delta}{\Sigma; \vec{\Delta}; \Delta \vdash \Tgoto{l}}
\]
\[
  \frac{\Sigma; \Delta \vdash a : \Tflat{\Tsum} \quad \Delta' \sqsubseteq \Delta \quad
        \Delta_{l_1} = \Delta_{l_2} = \Delta', \TAny}
       {\Sigma; \vec{\Delta}; \Delta \vdash
        \Tcase{a}{|\Delta'|}{l_1}{l_2}}
  \qquad
  \frac{\Sigma; \Delta_0, \ldots, \Delta_{l-1}; \Delta_l \vdash t_l \ (l < j) \quad
        \Sigma; \vec{\Delta}; \Delta \vdash t}
       {\Sigma; \Delta \vdash (t_0, \ldots, t_{j-1};\; t)}
\]
Here $\Delta' \sqsubseteq \Delta$ says that $\Delta'$ is a prefix of
$\Delta$. A definition's body $b_k$ starts in the frame
$\Tflat{\Ttup{n_k}}, \TAny$, and main's in the empty frame.

%===============================================================================
\section{Compilation}\label{sec:t-passes}

The passes of the typed pipeline are in \texttt{agda/Compiler}. Most are
the untyped passes (Part~II) on typed terms, and preserve types by
construction; what differs is said below. In each pass, the clauses not
shown are homomorphic.

\subsection{Cast Insertion (ISWIM \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{ISWIM}$}{ISWIM-T})}

Cast insertion turns every term of ISWIM into a term of type $\TAny$. A value
is injected with its flat type. An operation projects its operand to the
flat type it needs, at the point where the ISWIM machine checks it: the
operator of an application before the operand is evaluated. A primitive
checks both operands after it has evaluated them both, so they are bound
by $\key{let}$s first.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{cast}(x) &= x \\
  \pass{cast}(\lam{x} N) &= \Tinject{\Tfun}(\lam{x} \pass{cast}(N)) \\
  \pass{cast}(L\;M) &= (\Tproject{\Tfun} \pass{cast}(L))\; \pass{cast}(M) \\
  \pass{cast}(c) &= \Tinject{B} c \qquad c \text{ of base type } B \\
  \pass{cast}(\tuple{M_1,\ldots,M_n}) &= \Tinject{\Ttup{n}}
    \tuple{\pass{cast}(M_1),\ldots,\pass{cast}(M_n)} \\
  \pass{cast}(\prj{i} M) &= \prj{i} (\Tproject{\Tttup{n}{i}} \pass{cast}(M))
    \qquad \prj{i} \text{ of arity } n \\
  \pass{cast}(\inl M) &= \Tinject{\Tsum} (\inl \pass{cast}(M))
    \qquad\text{and likewise for } \key{inr} \\
  \pass{cast}(\Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N}) &=
    \Case{\Tproject{\Tsum} \pass{cast}(L)}{x}{\pass{cast}(M)}{y}{\pass{cast}(N)} \\
  \pass{cast}(\prim{p}{L}{M}) &= \Let x = \pass{cast}(L) \In \Let y = \pass{cast}(M) \In \\
    &\qquad \Tinject{\Tout{p}} \prim{p}{\Tproject{\Tint} x}{\Tproject{\Tint} y}
    \qquad x, y \text{ fresh} \\
  \pass{cast}(\Let x = M \In N) &= \Let x = \pass{cast}(M) \In \pass{cast}(N)
\end{align*}
The pass is defined on well-scoped terms, so every variable becomes a
typed one; a closed program becomes a closed term of type $\TAny$.

\subsection{CC: Enclose (\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{ISWIM}$}{ISWIM-T} \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos2}$}{Clos2-T})}

Typed enclose makes each function a closure that captures every variable
in scope, in order, so its body is unchanged. (The untyped pass captures
only the free variables; here pruning is optimize's job.)
\begin{align*}
  \pass{enclose}(\lam{y} N) &= \clos{x_1\ldots x_k}{x_1\ldots x_k\, y} \pass{enclose}(N)
    \qquad\text{where } x_1, \ldots, x_k \text{ are the variables in scope}
\end{align*}

\subsection{CC: Optimize (\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos2}$}{Clos2-T} \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos2}$}{Clos2-T})}

Optimize removes the captured values that the code does not use. Closures
are strict (they evaluate their free variables, which may err or diverge),
so a captured term is dropped only when the code does not use its name and
it is a variable, which is all enclose captures.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{optimize}(\clos{M_1\ldots M_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N) &=
    \clos{\pass{optimize}(M_{i_1})\ldots \pass{optimize}(M_{i_k})}{x_{i_1}\ldots x_{i_k}\, y}
    \pass{optimize}(N) \\
  \text{where } \{ i_1 < \cdots < i_k \} &=
    \{ i \mid x_i \in \FV(N) \text{ or } M_i \text{ is not a variable} \}
\end{align*}

\subsection{CC: Concretize (\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos2}$}{Clos2-T} \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos3}$}{Clos3-T})}

As the untyped pass: each closure stores its captured values in one tuple
$e$, of type $\Tflat{\Ttup{n}}$, and inside its code the name $x_i$
becomes $\prj{i} e$. The map $\varphi$ sends each $\Tl{Clos2}$ variable to a
reference $r ::= x \mid \prj{i} x$ in $\Tl{Clos3}$.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{concretize}_\varphi(x) &= \varphi(x) \\
  \pass{concretize}_\varphi(\clos{M_1\ldots M_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N) &=
    \clos{\pass{concretize}_\varphi(M_1)\ldots\pass{concretize}_\varphi(M_n)}{e\,y}
       \pass{concretize}_{\varphi_n}(N) \\
  \text{where } \varphi_n &= \{ y \mapsto y,\; x_1 \mapsto \prj{1} e,\; \ldots,\; x_n \mapsto \prj{n} e \},
    \quad e \text{ fresh}
\end{align*}
and under a $\key{let}$ or $\key{case}$ the bound variable maps to itself,
$\ext{\varphi}{x}{x}$; a program is concretized with $\varphi = \emptyset$.

\subsection{CC: Delay (\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos3}$}{Clos3-T} \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos4}$}{Clos4-T})}

A closure becomes a packed pair of its code and the tuple of its captured
values, and an application opens the closure and calls its code. Unlike the
untyped pass, which builds a pair and takes it apart with a $\key{let}$,
$\key{fst}$ and $\key{snd}$, the typed pass needs no $\key{let}$: $\key{callc}$
does it all.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{delay}(\clos{M_1\ldots M_n}{e\,y} N) &=
    \Tpack{\Tcodetm{e}{y}{\pass{delay}(N)}}{\tuple{\pass{delay}(M_1),\ldots,\pass{delay}(M_n)}} \\
  \pass{delay}(L\;M) &= \Tcallc{\pass{delay}(L)}{\pass{delay}(M)}
\end{align*}

\subsection{CC: Globalize (\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos4}$}{Clos4-T} \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos5}$}{Clos5-T})}

As the untyped pass: the code of every function becomes a global
definition, and the code itself a reference to it. The pass threads the
definitions made so far, from left to right; for code, it first globalizes
the body, so the functions nested in it become definitions first. Each
definition is typed over the table of the definitions older than it, and
the term made for an operand over the table made so far; both are weakened
to the bigger table at the end, which has an entry $n$ for code of type
$\Tcode{n}$.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{glob}(x, \vec{d}) &= (x, \vec{d}) \\
  \pass{glob}(\Tcodetm{e}{y}{N}, \vec{d}) &= (\funref{|\vec{d}_1|},\; \vec{d}_1, \Tcodetm{e}{y}{N'})
     \quad\text{where } (N', \vec{d}_1) = \pass{glob}(N, \vec{d}) \\
  \pass{glob}(\mathit{op}(\bar{x}_1.A_1, \ldots, \bar{x}_n.A_n), \vec{d}_0)
     &= (\mathit{op}(\bar{x}_1.A'_1, \ldots, \bar{x}_n.A'_n), \vec{d}_n)
     \quad\text{where } (A'_i, \vec{d}_i) = \pass{glob}(A_i, \vec{d}_{i-1}) \\[1ex]
  \pass{globalize}(M) &= (\vec{d};\; M') \quad\text{where } (M', \vec{d}) = \pass{glob}(M, \epsilon)
\end{align*}

\subsection{Remove Complex Operands (\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos5}$}{Clos5-T} \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Mon}$}{Mon-T})}

As the untyped pass: every complex operand is bound to a fresh variable by
a $\key{let}$, from left to right, so the operands of every operation
become atoms. The fresh variable has the type of the operand, which may be
any type (code, an environment, a closure, \ldots), hence $\Tl{Mon}$'s
$\key{let}$ at any type. A $\key{case}$ or $\key{let}$ is complex: its
branches and body are translated in place, so nothing is duplicated.
An operand that is an atom ($x$, $c$ or $\funref{k}$) stays in place;
any other operand $M$ is translated and bound to a fresh variable:
\begin{align*}
  \pass{atomize}(M) &= (\epsilon;\; M) \qquad M \text{ an atom} \\
  \pass{atomize}(M) &= (x{=}\pass{rco}(M);\; x) \qquad \text{otherwise, } x \text{ fresh} \\
  \pass{atomize}(M_1, \ldots, M_n) &= (\vec{b}_1 \cdots \vec{b}_n;\; a_1, \ldots, a_n)
    \quad\text{where } (\vec{b}_i; a_i) = \pass{atomize}(M_i) \\
  \pass{lets}(x_1{=}N_1 \cdots x_k{=}N_k,\; N) &= \Let x_1 = N_1 \In \cdots \Let x_k = N_k \In N
\end{align*}
The operations with operands are
\begin{multline*}
  \mathit{op}(M_1, \ldots, M_n) \in \{\, \Tpack{M}{N},\ \Tcallc{M}{N},\
     \tuple{M_1, \ldots, M_n},\ \prj{i} M,\ \inl M,\ \inr M, \\
     \prim{p}{M}{N},\ \Tinject{G} M,\ \Tproject{t} M \,\}
\end{multline*}
\begin{align*}
  \pass{rco}(a) &= a \qquad a \text{ an atom} \\
  \pass{rco}(\mathit{op}(M_1, \ldots, M_n)) &= \pass{lets}(\vec{b}, \mathit{op}(a_1, \ldots, a_n)) \\
  &\qquad \text{where } (\vec{b}; a_1, \ldots, a_n) = \pass{atomize}(M_1, \ldots, M_n) \\
  \pass{rco}(\Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N}) &= \pass{lets}(\vec{b}, \Case{a}{x}{\pass{rco}(M)}{y}{\pass{rco}(N)}) \\
  &\qquad \text{where } (\vec{b}; a) = \pass{atomize}(L) \\
  \pass{rco}(\Let x = M \In N) &= \Let x = \pass{rco}(M) \In \pass{rco}(N) \\
  \pass{rco}((d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)) &= (\pass{rco}(d_0), \ldots, \pass{rco}(d_{m-1});\; \pass{rco}(M)) \\
  \pass{rco}(\Tcodetm{e}{y}{N}) &= \Tcodetm{e}{y}{\pass{rco}(N)}
\end{align*}
Typed terms are well scoped, so the untyped pass's caveat about unbound
variables does not arise.

\subsection{Lift Locals (\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Mon}$}{Mon-T} \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Imp}$}{Imp-T})}

A $\key{let}$ becomes an assignment to the next slot of the frame, and the
variable a $\key{case}$ binds is the slot its payload is assigned to. The
frame of a body is typed by its context, and a variable is the slot at its
level, $\ell(x)$, the number of variables bound before it (in a definition
$\Tcodetm{e}{y}{N}$, $\ell(e) = 0$ and $\ell(y) = 1$). So the variables and
atoms stay as they are, and what the untyped pass computes (the slot of
each variable, the size of the frame) is in the types; select instructions
reads the slots' levels off them. With $\Gamma$ the variables in scope,
\begin{align*}
  \pass{lift}(x) &= \ell(x) \\
  \pass{lift}(\Let x = M \In N) &= \assign{|\Gamma|}{\pass{lift}(M)}{\pass{lift}(N)} \\
  \pass{lift}(\Case{a}{x}{M}{y}{N}) &=
    \Casei{\pass{lift}(a)}{|\Gamma|}{\pass{lift}(M)}{\pass{lift}(N)} \\
  \pass{lift}(\Tcodetm{e}{y}{N}) &= \pass{lift}(N) \\
  \pass{lift}((d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)) &=
    (\pass{lift}(d_0), \ldots, \pass{lift}(d_{m-1});\; \pass{lift}(M))
\end{align*}
and every other construct is a simple expression, unchanged.

\subsection{Explicate Control (\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Imp}$}{Imp-T} \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{CFG}$}{CFG-T})}

The equations are those of the untyped pass: $\pass{tail}(e)$ translates
$e$ in tail position, $\pass{assign}(e, s, k)$ translates ``slot $s := e$,
then the tail $k$'' in continuation-passing style, and a $\key{case}$ on
the right of an assignment adds $k$ as a block, the join point, which
both branches jump to. $\pass{add}(t)$ adds the block $t$ and gives its
label. Both thread
the blocks made so far, each typed over the older ones, so a new block's
label is the newest, and a label made earlier stays valid as more blocks are
added. In $\pass{assign}(e, s, k)$, $k$ starts in the frame $\Delta', A$
with $s = |\Delta'|$, $\Delta'$ a prefix of the frame $\Delta$ of $e$.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{tail}(r) &= \Treturn{r} \\
  \pass{tail}(\assign{s}{e_1}{e_2}) &= \pass{assign}(e_1, s, \pass{tail}(e_2)) \\
  \pass{tail}(\Casei{a}{s}{e_1}{e_2}) &= \Tcase{a}{s}{l_1}{l_2} \\
  &\qquad \text{where } l_1 = \pass{add}(\pass{tail}(e_1)),\ l_2 = \pass{add}(\pass{tail}(e_2)) \\[1ex]
  \pass{assign}(r, s, k) &= \assign{s}{r}{k} \\
  \pass{assign}(\assign{s'}{e_1}{e_2}, s, k) &= \pass{assign}(e_1, s', \pass{assign}(e_2, s, k)) \\
  \pass{assign}(\Casei{a}{s'}{e_1}{e_2}, s, k) &= \Tcase{a}{s'}{l_1}{l_2} \\
  &\qquad \text{where } l = \pass{add}(k),\
    l_i = \pass{add}(\pass{assign}(e_i, s, \Tgoto{l})) \\[1ex]
  \pass{explicate}(e) &= (\vec{t};\; t)
    \quad\text{where } t = \pass{tail}(e) \text{ and } \vec{t} \text{ are the blocks it adds}
\end{align*}
and $\pass{explicate}$ maps over the definitions and main of a program.
Unlike the untyped pass, a body carries no frame size. A $\key{case}$
in $\Tl{Imp}$ writes the next slot of its frame, so the $\key{case}$ tail
it becomes writes slot $|\Delta|$ (its $\Delta'$ is all of $\Delta$).

\subsection{Select Instructions (\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{CFG}$}{CFG-T} \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} X86)}

X86 stays untyped. A slot is the X86 variable at its level, and a reference
to a definition and a label are their levels too, which are the numbers X86
uses; so $\bar{s} = s$, $\bar{c} = c$, and $\overline{\funref{k}} =
\funref{k}$. An X86 value carries its own kind, so $\key{inject}$ is a
move; $\Tproject{t} a$ first runs $\Tcheck{t}$, an existing X86 instruction
that errs on a value of any other kind and otherwise only clobbers
$\rax$ or the flag. $\key{callc}$ opens the closure: its code and
environment are the two fields of a pair. The other simple expressions
and the tails are as in the untyped pass. With $d$ a location:
\begin{align*}
  \Tcheck{\Tint}(o) &= \key{movq}\; o, \rax;\ \key{subq}\; \$0, \rax &
  \Tcheck{\Tsum}(o) &= \key{testinl}\; o \\
  \Tcheck{\Tfun}(o) &= \key{load}\; \key{fst}\; o, \rax &
  \Tcheck{\Tttup{n}{i}}(o) &= \key{load}\; \pi_i\; o, \rax
\end{align*}
\begin{align*}
  \pass{sel}(a, d) &= \key{movq}\; \bar{a}, d \\
  \pass{sel}(\Tpack{a_1}{a_2}, d) &= \key{alloc}\; \key{pair}\; \bar{a}_1, \bar{a}_2, d \\
  \pass{sel}(\Tcallc{a_1}{a_2}, d) &= \key{load}\; \key{fst}\; \bar{a}_1, \rax;\
    \key{load}\; \key{snd}\; \bar{a}_1, \rdi;\ \key{movq}\; \bar{a}_2, \rsi; \\
  &\qquad \key{callq}\; \rax;\ \key{movq}\; \rax, d \\
  \pass{sel}(\tuple{a_1, \ldots, a_n}, d) &= \key{alloc}\; \key{tuple}\; \bar{a}_1, \ldots, \bar{a}_n, d \\
  \pass{sel}(\prj{i} a, d) &= \key{load}\; \pi_i\; \bar{a}, d \\
  \pass{sel}(\inl a, d) &= \key{alloc}\; \key{inl}\; \bar{a}, d
    \qquad\text{and likewise for } \key{inr} \\
  \pass{sel}(\key{sub}(a_1, a_2), d) &= \key{movq}\; \bar{a}_1, \rax;\
    \key{subq}\; \bar{a}_2, \rax;\ \key{movq}\; \rax, d \\
  \pass{sel}(\key{eq}(a_1, a_2), d) &= \key{cmpq}\; \bar{a}_2, \bar{a}_1;\ \key{sete}\; d \\
  \pass{sel}(\Tinject{G} a, d) &= \key{movq}\; \bar{a}, d \\
  \pass{sel}(\Tproject{t} a, d) &= \Tcheck{t}(\bar{a});\ \key{movq}\; \bar{a}, d \\[1ex]
  \pass{sel}(\Treturn{r}) &= \pass{sel}(r, \rax);\ \key{retq} \\
  \pass{sel}(\assign{s}{r}{t}) &= \pass{sel}(r, s);\ \pass{sel}(t) \\
  \pass{sel}(\Tgoto{l}) &= \key{jmp}\; l \\
  \pass{sel}(\Tcase{a}{s}{l_1}{l_2}) &=
    \key{testinl}\; \bar{a};\ \key{load}\; \key{payload}\; \bar{a}, s;\ \key{je}\; l_1, l_2
\end{align*}
The frame of a body has as many slots as the deepest level its tails
write: with $\Delta$ the frame a tail starts in,
\begin{align*}
  \mathit{depth}(\Treturn{r}) = \mathit{depth}(\Tgoto{l}) &= |\Delta| \\
  \mathit{depth}(\assign{s}{r}{t}) &= \max(|\Delta|, \mathit{depth}(t)) \\
  \mathit{depth}(\Tcase{a}{s}{l_1}{l_2}) &= \max(|\Delta|, s + 1) \\
  \pass{select}((t_0, \ldots, t_{j-1};\; t)) &=
    (\max(\mathit{depth}(t_0), \ldots, \mathit{depth}(t_{j-1}), \mathit{depth}(t));\
     \pass{sel}(t_0), \ldots, \pass{sel}(t_{j-1});\ \pass{sel}(t))
\end{align*}
and the start of each definition begins with the prologue
$\key{movq}\; \rdi, 0;\ \key{movq}\; \rsi, 1$, as in the untyped pass,
which moves its environment and argument to slots $0$ and $1$; main has
no prologue.

\subsection{The Compiler}

The typed compiler goes from untyped ISWIM to X86; simplify stays on
untyped ISWIM, before cast insertion.
\[
  \pass{compile}_4 = \pass{delay} \circ \pass{concretize}_{\emptyset}
     \circ \pass{optimize} \circ \pass{enclose} \circ \pass{cast\text{-}closed} \circ \pass{simplify}
  \qquad
  \pass{compile} = \pass{globalize} \circ \pass{compile}_4
\]
\begin{align*}
  \pass{compile\text{-}mon} &= \pass{rco} \circ \pass{compile}
  &
  \pass{compile\text{-}imp} &= \pass{lift} \circ \pass{compile\text{-}mon} \\
  \pass{compile\text{-}cfg} &= \pass{explicate} \circ \pass{compile\text{-}imp}
  &
  \pass{compile\text{-}x86} &= \pass{select} \circ \pass{compile\text{-}cfg}
\end{align*}
Cast insertion needs a closed program, so the pipeline uses
$\pass{cast\text{-}closed}$:
\[
  \pass{cast\text{-}closed}(M) =
  \begin{cases}
    \pass{cast}(M) & \text{if } \FV(M) = \emptyset \\
    (\Tproject{\Tfun} U)\; U, \quad U = \Tinject{\Ttup{0}} \emptytup & \text{otherwise}
  \end{cases}
\]
A program that is not closed, which cast insertion cannot type, becomes one
that stops with an error at once, the application of an empty tuple. The
theorems are for closed programs, for which $\pass{cast\text{-}closed}$ is
$\pass{cast}$; simplify keeps programs closed
(Lemma~\ref{lem:simplify-closed}).

\paragraph{Where it doesn't match the Agda literally.}
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Variables} are typed de Bruijn indices in the Agda
  (\texttt{Framework/Membership.agda}): \texttt{Fin k} in $\Tl{ISWIM}$ and
  $\Tl{Clos2}$, whose contexts are numbers $k$ (all variables have type
  $\TAny$), and $\Gamma \ni A$ from $\Tl{Clos3}$ on.
\item \textbf{Contexts, frames, tables and label lists} are listed newest
  first in the Agda; here oldest first, so that slot $s$, definition $k$
  and label $l$ are the \emph{levels} (\texttt{lvl}) of the indices,
  which select instructions uses. In particular the frame of a definition
  is $\TAny, \Tflat{\Ttup{n}}$ in the Agda, and $\Delta' \sqsubseteq \Delta$ (prefix) is
  $\Delta' \mathrel{\sqsubseteq} \Delta$ on newest-first lists (suffix).
\item \textbf{Slots} written in $\Tl{Imp}$ and $\Tl{CFG}$ are implicit in the
  Agda: $\key{assign}$ and $\key{case}$ of $\Tl{Imp}$ write slot $|\Delta|$;
  $\key{seq}$ and $\key{case}$ of $\Tl{CFG}$ carry a proof
  $p : \Delta' \sqsubseteq \Delta$ and write slot $|\Delta'|$. The CFG
  assignment $\assign{s}{r}{t}$ is the Agda's \texttt{seq}.
\item \textbf{Tuple tags} $\Tttup{n}{i}$, for a tuple of $n \geq 1$
  components read at component $1 \leq i \leq n$, are the Agda's
  \texttt{ttup} $(n-1)$ $(i-1)$: \texttt{ttup m j} is for a tuple of
  $m + 1$ components, with $j : \mathtt{Fin}\,(m+1)$, so that it needs no
  proof that the tuple is not empty. Tuple indices run from $1$ to $n$
  here, from $0$ in the Agda. The tags $\Tfun$, $\Tsum$ and $\Tint$ are
  \texttt{tfun}, \texttt{tsum} and \texttt{tint}. $\key{Bool}$ is the
  Agda's blackboard-bold B.
\item \textbf{Cast insertion} is defined on terms with a proof that they are
  well scoped (\texttt{WS k M}), and carries a renaming of the variables,
  for the variable the $\key{let}$ of a primitive adds around its second
  operand. The program pass is \texttt{cast-program}; the test
  $\FV(M) = \emptyset$ of $\pass{cast\text{-}closed}$ is \texttt{ws? 0 M}.
\item \textbf{Optimize} carries a renaming; the kept positions are flags
  (\texttt{keeps}), the kept terms \texttt{select}, and the code is renamed
  by \texttt{squeeze} (\texttt{Framework/VecSelect.agda}), which sends a
  dropped (unused) variable to the argument. $x_i \in \FV(N)$ is
  \texttt{uses N (suc i)}.
\item \textbf{Concretize}'s references are \texttt{Ref}
  (\texttt{var}, \texttt{prj}); $\varphi$ for the code of a closure is
  \texttt{fv-ref}. \textbf{Delay} carries a renaming, the identity for a
  program.
\item \textbf{Globalize} threads the definitions made, each over the table
  of the older ones (\texttt{Made}), and returns with each term a proof that
  the table grew (\texttt{Out}); terms are weakened (\texttt{wk}) to the
  bigger table, and at the end every definition is weakened to the whole
  table (\texttt{close}).
\item \textbf{Remove complex operands} is in continuation-passing style:
  \texttt{atomize M $\rho$ k} gives the atom for $M$ to the continuation $k$,
  which takes a renaming to the context where the atom is used (the lets of
  later operands put it under more binders); there are no lists
  $\vec{b}$, and the fresh variables are de Bruijn indices.
\item \textbf{Explicate control}'s results are \texttt{Out} records: a tail
  with the blocks made and a proof that the list of blocks grew;
  $\pass{add}$ is $\triangleright$ (\texttt{bs} $\triangleright$ \texttt{t}), and a
  body is \texttt{body bs t}.
\item \textbf{Select instructions}' $\mathit{depth}$ of a body is
  \texttt{blocks-depth bs $\sqcup$ depth t}; the prologue is
  \texttt{prologue}, added by \texttt{select-defs}.
\item \textbf{$\Tl{Mon}$} has its own syntax (\texttt{Atm}, \texttt{Exp});
  its meaning is the embedding $\Tembed{\cdot}$ into $\Tl{Clos5}$.
\end{itemize}


%===============================================================================
\section{Denotational: Graph Model}\label{sec:t-den}

The typed languages (Section~\ref{sec:t-langs}) are given meaning in the
graph model of Part~II: the same domain $\V$, the same operators
(Section~\ref{sec:den-ops}) and consistency, and the same clauses, with
one new clause each for $\Tinject{G}$ and $\Tproject{t}$
(\texttt{agda/Semantics/Graph/Typed/}). A value carries its own kind, so
the domain needs no type tags. Since the syntax is intrinsically typed,
each semantics is defined by plain structural recursion on typed terms,
with no ABT; the generic infrastructure
(\texttt{agda/Framework/GraphModel/Typed*.agda},
\texttt{agda/proof/Framework/GraphModel/Typed*.agda}) provides the
guard and the kind check that project uses, and the facts from which a
language proves its denotations monotone, consistent and continuous
($\mathit{Ok}$) by induction on the term, one line per construct, with
the laws of the binders and of a closure call. The lemmas of each language are in
\texttt{agda/proof/Semantics/Graph/Typed/}. X86's semantics is that of
Part~II (Section~\ref{sec:den-x86}), unchanged.

We write variables by name, as in Part~II; in the Agda a variable is a
typed de~Bruijn index, and an environment $\rho : \mathbb{N} \to \SET{\V}$
gives position $i$ the meaning of the variable with index $i$, so an
environment, like the domain, carries no types. A variable not in scope
means $\{\omega\}$, as in $\rho_0$; in a typed term there is none, but the
body of a closed code is given the environment $\rho_0$ extended with its
parameters.

\subsection{Project and the typed operators}

$\Tinject{G}$ is the identity, and $\Tproject{t}$ keeps its operand when
the operand has a value that the tag $t$ accepts, and means $\emptyset$
otherwise: a failed project means $\emptyset$, as any error does in the
graph model. The tags are the flat types that project can test: a
function, a sum, an integer, and component $i$ of a tuple of $n+1$
components, written $\key{fun}$, $\key{sum}$, $\key{int}$ and
$\key{tup}_{n+1,i}$; each is what the X86 test of the tag reads. A
function is an entry of a function's graph up to $\Tl{Clos3}$
($\Tfuntwo$), and a pair of code and environment from $\Tl{Clos4}$ on,
where a closure is such a pair ($\Tfunfour$):
\begin{align*}
  \Tguard(P, D) &= \{ w \in D \mid \exists d \in D.\; P(d) \} \\[1ex]
  \Tfuntwo(d) &\iff d = V \mapsto w \text{ or } d = \nu \qquad
  \Tfunfour(d) \iff d = \pairv{u}{v} \\
  \Taccepts{F}(\key{fun}, d) &\iff F(d) \qquad
  \Taccepts{F}(\key{sum}, d) \iff d = \Left{u} \text{ or } d = \Right{u} \\
  \Taccepts{F}(\key{int}, d) &\iff d \text{ is an integer constant} \\
  \Taccepts{F}(\key{tup}_{n+1,i}, d) &\iff d = \tup{i}{u} \text{ for some } u \text{ (a tuple of } n+1 \text{ components)} \\[1ex]
  \key{project}^{F}_t(D) &= \Tguard(\Taccepts{F}(t, -), D)
\end{align*}
So $\key{project}^{F}_t(D)$ is all of $D$, not only the elements that $t$
accepts. The semantics of a typed language $\ell$ has the clauses
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\Tinject{G} M}^{\ell}\rho &= \SEM{M}^{\ell}\rho \\
  \SEM{\Tproject{t} M}^{\ell}\rho &= \key{project}^{\Tfuntwo}_t(\SEM{M}^{\ell}\rho)
    \qquad (\ell \in \{\Tl{ISWIM}, \Tl{2}, \Tl{3}\}) \\
  \SEM{\Tproject{t} M}^{\ell}\rho &= \key{project}^{\Tfunfour}_t(\SEM{M}^{\ell}\rho)
    \qquad (\ell \in \{\Tl{4}, \Tl{5}\},\ \text{and the simple expressions of } \Tl{Imp} \text{ and } \Tl{CFG})
\end{align*}
The guard is monotone, consistent (its result is part of its operand)
and continuous (an element and its witness come from one finite
approximation). All the other operators are those of
Section~\ref{sec:den-ops}, and the operations of a typed term are
applied only to operands of their type, but the semantics does not rely
on it: an operator on a value of the wrong kind gives $\emptyset$, as in
Part~II.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{ISWIM}$}{ISWIM-T}, \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos2}$}{Clos2-T} and \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos3}$}{Clos3-T}}

$\SEM{-}^{\Tl{ISWIM}}$ is $\SEM{-}^{\mathrm{ISWIM}}$: the common clauses
of Part~II and those for $\lambda$ and application, together with the
clauses for inject and project above. $\Tl{Clos2}$ and $\Tl{Clos3}$ are
as Clos2 and Clos3, except that a closure captures the values of terms
$M_1, \ldots, M_n$ (of type $\TAny$), not only variables:
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\clos{M_1\ldots M_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N}^{\Tl{2}}\rho &=
    \key{guard}(D_1, \ldots, D_n;\;
       \Lambda(\lambda Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\Tl{2}}\rho_0(x_1{:=}D_1,\ldots,x_n{:=}D_n, y{:=}Y))) \\
    &\qquad\text{where } D_i = \SEM{M_i}^{\Tl{2}}\rho \\
  \SEM{\clos{M_1\ldots M_n}{e\, y} N}^{\Tl{3}}\rho &=
    \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \Lambda(\lambda Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\Tl{3}}\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y))) \\
    &\qquad \app \mathcal{T}(\SEM{M_1}^{\Tl{3}}\rho, \ldots, \SEM{M_n}^{\Tl{3}}\rho)
\end{align*}
where in $\Tl{Clos3}$ the code's parameters have the types
$e : \Tflat{\key{tup}\;n}$ and $y : \TAny$, and application is $\app$.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos4}$}{Clos4-T} and \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos5}$}{Clos5-T}}

In $\Tl{Clos4}$ code (of type $\Tcode{n}$) is a closed function of two
arguments, the environment and the argument; a closure (of type
$\Tflat{\key{fun}}$) is the pair of code and environment, built by
$\key{pack}$; and $\key{callc}$ applies the closure's code ($\key{car}$)
to its environment ($\key{cdr}$) and then to the argument. There is no
$\key{fst}$ or $\key{snd}$ in the syntax: only a call opens a closure.
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\Tcodetm{e}{y}{N}}^{\Tl{4}}\rho &= \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \Lambda(\lambda Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\Tl{4}}\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y))) \\
  \SEM{\Tpack{L}{M}}^{\Tl{4}}\rho &= \key{pair}(\SEM{L}^{\Tl{4}}\rho, \SEM{M}^{\Tl{4}}\rho) \\
  \SEM{\Tcallc{L}{M}}^{\Tl{4}}\rho &= (\key{car}(D) \app \key{cdr}(D)) \app \SEM{M}^{\Tl{4}}\rho
    \quad\text{where } D = \SEM{L}^{\Tl{4}}\rho
\end{align*}
$\Tl{Clos5}$ is $\Tl{Clos4}$ with code replaced by a reference $\funref{f}$
to a global definition. A reference is typed: $f : \Sigma \ni n$ picks a
definition, of $n$ free variables, from the list $\Sigma$ of the arities of
the program's definitions. So a table is indexed by the typed references
into $\Sigma$, not by numbers. As in Part~II, each definition $d_k$ (the
oldest is $d_0$; the Agda list is newest first) is given meaning with the
table of the older ones, found by the level of the reference, and main
with the table of all of them:
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\funref{f}}^{\Tl{5}}_T\rho &= T(f) \qquad (T : \forall n.\; \Sigma \ni n \to \SET{\V}) \\
  \key{code}_T(N) &= \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \Lambda(\lambda Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\Tl{5}}_T\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y))) \\
  \key{table}(d_0,\ldots,d_{m-1})(f) &=
    \begin{cases}
      \key{code}_{\key{table}(d_0,\ldots,d_{k-1})}(d_k) & \text{the level of } f \text{ is } k < m \\
      \emptyset & \text{otherwise}
    \end{cases} \\
  \SEM{(d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)}^{\Tl{5}} &= \SEM{M}^{\Tl{5}}_{\key{table}(d_0,\ldots,d_{m-1})}\,\rho_0
\end{align*}
with the other clauses those of $\Tl{Clos4}$ under $T$.

\paragraph{$\Tl{Mon}$.} $\Tl{Mon}$ has no semantics of its own: it is a
syntax of its own (so that monadic form needs no proof), and a $\Tl{Mon}$
program $P$ means its embedding $\Tembed{P}$ into $\Tl{Clos5}$, which maps
each construct to the $\Tl{Clos5}$ construct of the same name and an atom
to a variable, a constant or a reference:
$\SEM{P}^{\Tl{Mon}} = \SEM{\Tembed{P}}^{\Tl{5}}$.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Imp}$}{Imp-T}, continuation-passing}

As Imp's continuation-passing semantics (Part~II), with these
differences. The frame is typed: its slots are typed variables, and an
assignment or a case binds a new slot, as a $\key{let}$ does in
$\Tl{Clos5}$, so a frame $\sigma$ is an environment, there is no frame
size $n$, no slot can be out of bounds, and the $\mathit{checked}_n$ of
Part~II is gone. An expression never changes the slots it can see, so a
continuation takes only the value of the expression, not a store. The
atoms and simple expressions are $\Tl{Clos5}$'s operators on atoms, with
$\mathcal{A}_\sigma(x) = \sigma(x)$, $\mathcal{A}_\sigma(c) =
\mathcal{B}(c)$, $\mathcal{A}_\sigma(\funref{f}) = T(f)$, and
\begin{align*}
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\Tpack{a_1}{a_2}) &= \key{pair}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_1), \mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_2)) \\
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\Tcallc{a_1}{a_2}) &= (\key{car}(A) \app \key{cdr}(A)) \app \mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_2)
    \quad\text{where } A = \mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_1) \\
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\Tinject{G} a) &= \mathcal{A}_\sigma(a) \qquad
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\Tproject{t} a) = \key{project}^{\Tfunfour}_t(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a))
\end{align*}
and tuples, projections, injections and primitives as in Part~II. With $T$
fixed, writing $\SEM{e}\sigma\,\kappa$ for $\SEM{e}^{\Tl{Imp}}_T\sigma\,\kappa$,
with $\kappa : \SET{\V} \to \SET{\V}$:
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{r}\sigma\,\kappa &= \kappa(\mathcal{S}_\sigma(r)) \\
  \SEM{\assign{x}{e_1}{e_2}}\sigma\,\kappa &= \SEM{e_1}\sigma\,(\lambda D.\,
    \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \SEM{e_2}\ext{\sigma}{x}{X}\,\kappa) \app D) \\
  \SEM{\Case{a}{x}{e_1}{x}{e_2}}\sigma\,\kappa &=
    \mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a),\; \lambda X.\, \SEM{e_1}\ext{\sigma}{x}{X}\,\kappa,\;
      \lambda X.\, \SEM{e_2}\ext{\sigma}{x}{X}\,\kappa) \\[1ex]
  \key{code}^{\mathrm{K}}_T(b) &= \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \Lambda(\lambda Y.\,
    \SEM{b}^{\Tl{Imp}}_T\,\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y)\,(\lambda D.\, D))) \\
  \SEM{(b_0, \ldots, b_{m-1};\; e)}^{\Tl{Imp}} &=
    \SEM{e}^{\Tl{Imp}}_{\key{table}^{\mathrm{K}}(b_0,\ldots,b_{m-1})}\,\rho_0\,(\lambda D.\, D)
\end{align*}
where the body $b$ of a definition has the parameters $e : \Tflat{\key{tup}\;n}$
and $y : \TAny$ (its first two slots), and $\key{table}^{\mathrm{K}}$ is
$\Tl{Clos5}$'s table with $\key{code}^{\mathrm{K}}$. There is no direct-style
semantics of $\Tl{Imp}$.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{CFG}$}{CFG-T}}

As CFG (Part~II), with typed frames as in $\Tl{Imp}$ and typed labels. A
label $l : \mathit{Bs} \ni \Delta$ is an index into the list of the
blocks a tail can see, and carries the context $\Delta$ of its block, so a
jump runs the block in the frame as it is, and there is no jump to a
missing block. An assignment $x \mathrel{:=}_{\Delta'} s;\; t$ and a
case forget the slots of a finished scope: they continue in the frame
cut to an older context $\Delta'$ of the current one, $\sigma|_{\Delta'}$,
extended with the new slot. With $T$ and the meanings $B$ of the visible
blocks fixed, writing $\SEM{t}\sigma$ for $\SEM{t}^{\Tl{CFG}}_{T,B}\sigma$:
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\key{return}\; s}\sigma &= \mathcal{S}_\sigma(s) \\
  \SEM{x \mathrel{:=}_{\Delta'} s;\; t}\sigma &=
    \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \SEM{t}\ext{(\sigma|_{\Delta'})}{x}{X}) \app \mathcal{S}_\sigma(s) \\
  \SEM{\key{goto}\; l}\sigma &= B(l)(\sigma) \\
  \SEM{\key{case}\;a\;\key{of}_{\Delta'}\;\key{goto}\;l_1 \mid \key{goto}\;l_2}\sigma &=
    \mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a),\;
      \lambda X.\, B(l_1)(\ext{(\sigma|_{\Delta'})}{x}{X}),\; \lambda X.\, B(l_2)(\ext{(\sigma|_{\Delta'})}{x}{X}))
\end{align*}
where $\mathcal{A}$ and $\mathcal{S}$ are $\Tl{Imp}$'s. The blocks of a
body are given meaning newest first, each with the meanings of the older
ones, which are all it can jump to; a body $(\vec{t};\; t)$ runs its
start $t$ with them ($\mathit{run}_T$); the code of a definition is
$\Lambda(\lambda X.\, \Lambda(\lambda Y.\, \mathit{run}_T(b)(\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y))))$,
and main runs in $\rho_0$, with $\Tl{Clos5}$'s table.

%===============================================================================
\section{The Graph Model with Errors}\label{sec:t-gerr}

The typed languages are also given meaning in the graph model with
errors of Part~II (Section~\ref{sec:graph-errors}), over the same error
monad: a set is a computation that errs when it holds $\omega$, and
$\bind$ runs a function on its values
(\texttt{agda/Semantics/GraphErr/Typed/},
\texttt{agda/Framework/GraphModel/ErrorMonad.agda}). The difference from
the untyped semantics is that \emph{only project checks kinds and errs}.
Each operator is the graph model's under the binds of its operands, in
the machines' order, with no $\key{checked}$: a typed operand has the
kind its operator needs. An operand may still hold $\omega$, from a
failed project, which the binds propagate.
\begin{align*}
  D \app^{\mathsf{T}} E &= D \bind \lambda F.\, E \bind \lambda X.\, F \app X \\
  \key{proj}^{\mathsf{T}}_i(D) &= D \bind \lambda X.\, \key{proj}_i(X) \\
  \mathcal{C}^{\mathsf{T}}(D, F, G) &= D \bind \lambda X.\, \mathcal{C}(X^{\checkmark}, F, G) \\
  \key{prim}^{\mathsf{T}}_p(D, E) &= D \bind \lambda X.\, E \bind \lambda Y.\, \key{prim}_p(X, Y) \\
  \SEM{\Let x = M \In N}^{\ell,\omega}\rho &= \SEM{M}^{\ell,\omega}\rho \bind \lambda X.\, \SEM{N}^{\ell,\omega}\ext{\rho}{x}{X}
\end{align*}
where $X^{\checkmark}$ keeps the injections of $X$ whose payload is not
$\omega$, as in Part~II. A $\key{let}$ is the bind of its body.
$\Lambda^{\omega}$, $\Lambda\Lambda^{\omega}$, $\mathcal{T}^{\omega}$,
$\key{inl}^{\omega}$, $\key{inr}^{\omega}$ and $\key{pair}^{\omega}$,
which check nothing, are those of Part~II. Variables and constants
are as in Part~II; inject is the identity.

\subsection{Project}

The check of the error monad is generalized to see the kinds of values
through a map $\varphi$ on kinds: with $\varphi$ the identity it is
$\key{checked}_k$ of Part~II
(\texttt{agda/Framework/GraphModel/TypedCheck.agda}). A project to tag $t$ binds
its operand, adds $\omega$ when the operand has a value of another kind
than that of the flat type $\Tflatof{t}$ of $t$, and keeps the values of
that kind, provided that the operand passes the test of $t$:
\begin{align*}
  \Tchecked{\varphi}_k(X, A) &= \{ \omega \mid \exists d \in X.\; \varphi(\Tkind(d)) \neq k \} \cup A \\
  \Tkeep{\varphi}_t(X) &= \{ d \in X \mid \Thit(t, X),\; \varphi(\Tkind(d)) = \Tkindf(\Tflatof{t}) \} \\
  \key{project}^{\varphi}_t(D) &= D \bind \lambda X.\, \Tchecked{\varphi}_{\Tkindf(\Tflatof{t})}(X, \Tkeep{\varphi}_t(X))
\end{align*}
where $\Tkindf$ is the kind of the values of a flat type (an integer, a
tuple of $n$ components, an injection, a function), and $\Thit(t, X)$
holds always but for a tuple tag, where it needs an entry $\tup{i}{e} \in X$
of $n+1$ components for the component $i$ that the tag (and the X86 test,
and the $\key{proj}_i$ after it) reads: a tuple's meaning may hold entries for
some components only. Keeping only the values of the right kind keeps a
denotation consistent. Up to $\Tl{Clos3}$, $\varphi$ is the identity
($\key{project}^{\mathsf{T}}_t$). From $\Tl{Clos4}$ on a closure is a pair, so
project sees a pair as a function and code, which is not a value of
type $\TAny$, as an error:
\[
  \Tkindfour(\mathit{pair}) = \mathit{fun} \qquad
  \Tkindfour(\mathit{fun}) = \mathit{err} \qquad
  \Tkindfour(k) = k \quad \text{otherwise}
\]
and $\Tl{Clos4}$, $\Tl{Clos5}$, $\Tl{Imp}$ and $\Tl{CFG}$ use
$\key{project}^{\Tkindfour}_t$.

\subsection{The languages}

$\SEM{-}^{\Tl{ISWIM},\omega}$ is $\SEM{-}^{\mathrm{ISWIM},\omega}$ with the
operators above: $\lambda$ is $\Lambda^{\omega}$, application
$\app^{\mathsf{T}}$, which binds the operator and then the argument and
checks nothing; tuples, projections, case and primitives are
$\mathcal{T}^{\omega}$, $\key{proj}^{\mathsf{T}}_i$, $\mathcal{C}^{\mathsf{T}}$ and
$\key{prim}^{\mathsf{T}}_p$; and project is $\key{project}^{\mathsf{T}}_t$. A
closure of $\Tl{Clos2}$ binds its captured terms left to right; one of
$\Tl{Clos3}$ binds the tuple of them, and is its code applied to it:
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\clos{M_1\ldots M_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N}^{\Tl{2},\omega}\rho &=
    (D_1, \ldots, D_n) \bind \lambda X_1 \ldots X_n.\, \\
    &\qquad \Lambda^{\omega}(\lambda Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\Tl{2},\omega}\rho_0(x_1{:=}X_1,\ldots,x_n{:=}X_n, y{:=}Y)) \\
  \SEM{\clos{M_1\ldots M_n}{e\, y} N}^{\Tl{3},\omega}\rho &=
    \mathcal{T}^{\omega}(D_1, \ldots, D_n) \bind \lambda E.\, \\
    &\qquad \Lambda\Lambda^{\omega}(\lambda X\, Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\Tl{3},\omega}\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y)) \app E
\end{align*}
where $D_i = \SEM{M_i}^{\ell,\omega}\rho$. In $\Tl{Clos4}$, code is
$\Lambda\Lambda^{\omega}$, $\key{pack}$ is $\key{pair}^{\omega}$, and a call
binds the closure and then the argument, and checks nothing (in
particular not that the closure is a pair):
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\Tcodetm{e}{y}{N}}^{\Tl{4},\omega}\rho &= \Lambda\Lambda^{\omega}(\lambda X\, Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\Tl{4},\omega}\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y)) \\
  \SEM{\Tpack{L}{M}}^{\Tl{4},\omega}\rho &= \key{pair}^{\omega}(\SEM{L}^{\Tl{4},\omega}\rho, \SEM{M}^{\Tl{4},\omega}\rho) \\
  \SEM{\Tcallc{L}{M}}^{\Tl{4},\omega}\rho &= \SEM{L}^{\Tl{4},\omega}\rho \bind \lambda P.\,
    \SEM{M}^{\Tl{4},\omega}\rho \bind \lambda X.\, (\key{car}(P) \app \key{cdr}(P)) \app X
\end{align*}

\paragraph{$\Tl{Clos5}$ and $\Tl{Mon}$.} As $\Tl{Clos4}$, with references
to global definitions. Unlike the graph model's, the table of the
semantics with errors is indexed by the level of a reference, a number,
as in Part~II, and gives $\key{errcode}$ at any other position:
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\funref{f}}^{\Tl{5},\omega}_T\rho &= T(\mathit{lvl}(f)) \qquad
  \key{code}^{\omega}_T(N) = \Lambda\Lambda^{\omega}(\lambda X\, Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\Tl{5},\omega}_T\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y)) \\
  \key{table}^{\omega}(d_0,\ldots,d_{m-1})(k) &=
    \begin{cases}
      \key{code}^{\omega}_{\key{table}^{\omega}(d_0,\ldots,d_{k-1})}(d_k) & k < m \\
      \key{errcode} & \text{otherwise}
    \end{cases}
\end{align*}
The table is generic in what the definitions are, and is also that of
$\Tl{Imp}$ and $\Tl{CFG}$. A $\Tl{Mon}$ program means its embedding into
$\Tl{Clos5}$, $\SEM{\Tembed{P}}^{\Tl{5},\omega}$.

\paragraph{$\Tl{Imp}$ and $\Tl{CFG}$.} The frame is the environment, so,
as in the graph model, it needs no checks: there is no
$\mathit{checked}^{\omega}_n$. An atom and a simple expression mean what
they mean as $\Tl{Mon}$ expressions, that is, in $\Tl{Clos5}$:
$\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a) = \SEM{\Tembed{a}}^{\Tl{5},\omega}_T\sigma$ and
$\mathcal{S}_\sigma(s) = \SEM{\Tembed{s}}^{\Tl{5},\omega}_T\sigma$. A slot is
bound as the X86 machine writes it, by the application of a function:
\begin{align*}
  \Tletv(D, F) &= D \bind \lambda X.\, \Lambda^{\omega}(F) \app X \\
  \SEM{\assign{x}{e_1}{e_2}}\sigma\,\kappa &= \SEM{e_1}\sigma\,(\lambda D.\,
    \Tletv(D,\; \lambda X.\, \SEM{e_2}\ext{\sigma}{x}{X}\,\kappa)) \\
  \SEM{\Case{a}{x}{e_1}{x}{e_2}}\sigma\,\kappa &=
    \mathcal{C}^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a),\; \lambda X.\, \SEM{e_1}\ext{\sigma}{x}{X}\,\kappa,\;
      \lambda X.\, \SEM{e_2}\ext{\sigma}{x}{X}\,\kappa) \\[1ex]
  \SEM{x \mathrel{:=}_{\Delta'} s;\; t}\sigma &=
    \Tletv(\mathcal{S}_\sigma(s),\; \lambda X.\, \SEM{t}\ext{(\sigma|_{\Delta'})}{x}{X}) \\
  \SEM{\key{case}\;a\;\key{of}_{\Delta'}\;\key{goto}\;l_1 \mid \key{goto}\;l_2}\sigma &=
    \mathcal{C}^{\mathsf{T}}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a),\;
      \lambda X.\, B(l_1)(\ext{(\sigma|_{\Delta'})}{x}{X}),\; \lambda X.\, B(l_2)(\ext{(\sigma|_{\Delta'})}{x}{X}))
\end{align*}
with $\SEM{r}\sigma\,\kappa = \kappa(\mathcal{S}_\sigma(r))$,
$\SEM{\key{return}\; s}\sigma = \mathcal{S}_\sigma(s)$ and
$\SEM{\key{goto}\; l}\sigma = B(l)(\sigma)$; a label indexes a block that is
there, so a jump needs no check. The code of a definition is
$\Lambda\Lambda^{\omega}$ of its body on $\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y)$ (with the
continuation $\lambda D.\, D$ in $\Tl{Imp}$), the blocks are given meaning
as in the graph model, and main runs in $\rho_0$ with
$\key{table}^{\omega}$.

\subsection{Select: X86 and checked \texorpdfstring{$\Tl{CFG}$}{CFG-T}}

Every pass of the typed pipeline but delay and select preserves the
meaning of a program exactly ($\simeq$) in the graph model with errors;
in the graph model, select does too. Select, with errors, is the one
pass whose correctness is not an equation, unlike in Part~II: X86's
semantics (Section~\ref{sec:graph-errors}) checks the kind of every
operand, so the code of a definition has error entries at inputs of the
wrong kind, where the code of $\Tl{CFG}$, which checks nothing but
project, has no entries at all. The two programs err together, produce
the same constants, and produce a value together:
\begin{align*}
  \Same(D, E) &\iff (\omega \in D \iff \omega \in E) \land
    (\forall c.\; c \in D \iff c \in E) \land (\mathit{val}(D) \iff \mathit{val}(E)) \\
  &\Same(\SEM{P}^{\Tl{CFG},\omega},\; \SEM{\pass{select}(P)}^{\mathrm{X86},\omega})
    \qquad \text{for every } \Tl{CFG} \text{ program } P,
\end{align*}
and so the end-to-end theorems of the typed pipeline relate the meaning
of the source to that of each target by $\Same$. The proof
(\texttt{agda/proof/Correctness/GraphErr/SelectCorrect.agda}) goes
through $\Tl{CFG}$ with the checks of X86
(\texttt{SelectCheck.agda}): the typed syntax given the untyped, checking
operators that the selected instructions mean ($\key{call}^{\omega}$ on the
$\key{car}^{\omega}$ and $\key{cdr}^{\omega}$ of a closure,
$\key{proj}^{\omega}_i$, $\key{prim}^{\omega}_p$, $\mathcal{C}^{\omega}$, a slot
bound by $\Lambda^{\omega}$ and $\app^{\omega}$), where $\Tproject{t}\,a$ runs the X86
test of $t$ and then moves $a$, and the code of a definition binds its
parameters to its first two slots, as the X86 prologue does. X86 means
exactly ($\simeq$) what $\Tl{CFG}$ with checks does
(\texttt{SelectX86.agda}), and $\Tl{CFG}$ with checks is related to
$\Tl{CFG}$ in both directions (\texttt{SelectReflect.agda},
\texttt{SelectPreserve.agda}) by a relation that is the identity on
errors, constants and first-order data, and relates code by what it
does on inputs of the kind its pair says, so that an entry at inputs of
the wrong kind is related to anything.


%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{CEK Machines}\label{sec:t-cek}

The machines of the typed languages (\texttt{agda/Semantics/CEK/Typed})
are those of Part~II (Section~\ref{sec:machines}) with typed values,
environments and continuations. A continuation is indexed by the type
of the value it expects, a state $\ev{M}{\rho}{\kappa}$ or
$\rt{v}{\kappa}$ pairs a term or value of type $A$ with a continuation
expecting $A$, and an environment is typed by the context of its term,
so a state is well typed by construction. The machines are machines in
the sense of Part~II: step functions that move to the next state or
stop with an observation (Section~\ref{sec:obs}).

Every transition of Part~II that stops with $\error$ on a value of the
wrong kind (an operator that is not a closure, a $\prj{i}$ of a
non-tuple, a $\key{case}$ of a non-injection, a primitive on a
non-integer, an unbound variable, a missing definition or block) has no
counterpart: those states cannot be written. Each typed machine stops
with $\error$ in exactly one way, a $\Tproject{t}$ of a value whose tag
is not the flat type $\Tflatof{t}$ that $t$ tests.

\subsection{Values, continuations and shared transitions}\label{sec:t-cek-shared}

A value of type $\TAny$ is a value of a flat type $G$ tagged with $G$,
$\TdynV{G}{v}$; the components of tuples and injections, and the
values in environments of variables of type $\TAny$, have type $\TAny$.
The flat types $G$ are an integer (a base type), $\key{tup}\;n$,
$\key{sum}$ and $\key{fun}$; a project's tag $t$ tests one of
$\key{fun}$, $\key{sum}$, the integers, or a tuple of $n+1$ components
($\Tflatof{t}$).
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  v &::=& \cdots \mid c \mid \tupV{v_1, \ldots, v_n} \mid \inlV v \mid \inrV v \mid \TdynV{G}{v} \\
  \kappa &::=& \cdots \mid \halt \mid \Kf{tupK}{\bar{v}, \bar{M}, \rho, \kappa} \mid \Kf{getK_i}{\kappa}
    \mid \Kf{inlK}{\kappa} \mid \Kf{inrK}{\kappa} \mid \Kf{caseK}{x.M, y.N, \rho, \kappa} \\
  &\mid& \Kf{primL_p}{M, \rho, \kappa} \mid \Kf{primR_p}{n, \kappa}
    \mid \Kf{letK}{x.N, \rho, \kappa} \mid \Kf{injK_G}{\kappa} \mid \Kf{projK_t}{\kappa}
\end{array}
\]
\begin{align*}
  \obs(\TdynV{G}{v}) &= \obs(v), \qquad \text{otherwise $\obs$ as in Part~II (closures, code and packs give $\key{fun}$)} \\
  \Tres{n} &= n, \quad
  \Tres{\mathsf{true}} = \inlV \TdynV{\key{tup}\,0}{\tupV{}}, \quad
  \Tres{\mathsf{false}} = \inrV \TdynV{\key{tup}\,0}{\tupV{}} \\
  \Mach{\ell}(M) &= (\mathit{State}^{\ell},\; \step{\ell},\; \ev{M}{\emptyset}{\halt})
    \qquad (\ell \in \{\Tl{ISWIM}, \Tl{Clos2}, \Tl{Clos3}, \Tl{Clos4}\})
\end{align*}
The primitive $\key{eq}$ gives an injection of the empty tuple tagged
with $\key{tup}\;0$. $\tnext$ is that of
Section~\ref{sec:machine-shared}. The transitions shared by the machines
of $\Tl{ISWIM}$ to $\Tl{Clos5}$ are those of Part~II for variables,
constants, tuples, $\prj{i}$, $\key{inl}$, $\key{inr}$, $\key{case}$ and
primitives, without the cases that stop with $\error$, together with
$\key{let}$ (now in every language) and the casts:
\[
\begin{array}{@{}l@{\;\mapsto\;}l@{\quad}l@{}}
  \ev{x}{\rho}{\kappa} & \nxt \rt{\rho(x)}{\kappa} \\
  \ev{(\Let x = M \In N)}{\rho}{\kappa} & \nxt \ev{M}{\rho}{\Kf{letK}{x.N, \rho, \kappa}} \\
  \ev{(\Tinject{G} M)}{\rho}{\kappa} & \nxt \ev{M}{\rho}{\Kf{injK_G}{\kappa}} \\
  \ev{(\Tproject{t} M)}{\rho}{\kappa} & \nxt \ev{M}{\rho}{\Kf{projK_t}{\kappa}} \\[1ex]
  \rt{\tupV{v_1, \ldots, v_n}}{\Kf{getK_i}{\kappa}} & \nxt \rt{v_i}{\kappa} \\
  \rt{(\inlV u)}{\Kf{caseK}{x.M, y.N, \rho, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{M}{\ext{\rho}{x}{u}}{\kappa} \\
  \rt{(\inrV u)}{\Kf{caseK}{x.M, y.N, \rho, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{N}{\ext{\rho}{y}{u}}{\kappa} \\
  \rt{n}{\Kf{primL_p}{M, \rho, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{M}{\rho}{\Kf{primR_p}{n, \kappa}} \\
  \rt{m}{\Kf{primR_p}{n, \kappa}} & \nxt \rt{\Tres{\delta(p, n, m)}}{\kappa} \\
  \rt{v}{\Kf{letK}{x.N, \rho, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{N}{\ext{\rho}{x}{v}}{\kappa} \\
  \rt{v}{\Kf{injK_G}{\kappa}} & \nxt \rt{\TdynV{G}{v}}{\kappa} \\
  \rt{\TdynV{H}{v}}{\Kf{projK_t}{\kappa}} & \nxt \rt{v}{\kappa} & H = \Tflatof{t} \\
