  & \stp \error & \text{otherwise}
\end{array}
\]
The last line is the only $\error$ of the machines. A variable is
always bound, $i$ is below the tuple's length, and a $\key{case}$ or
primitive always finds an injection or an integer, by typing.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{ISWIM}$}{ISWIM}}\label{sec:t-cek-iswim}

As in Part~II, without the $\error$ cases: an operator has type
$\Tflat{\key{fun}}$, so its value is a closure.
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  v &::=& \cloV{\lam{x} N}{\rho} \mid \cdots \\
  \kappa &::=& \Kf{appL}{M, \rho, \kappa} \mid \Kf{appR}{v, \kappa} \mid \cdots
\end{array}
\]
\[
\begin{array}{@{}l@{\;\mapsto\;}l@{\quad}l@{}}
  \ev{(\lam{x} N)}{\rho}{\kappa} & \nxt \rt{\cloV{\lam{x} N}{\rho}}{\kappa} \\
  \ev{(L\;M)}{\rho}{\kappa} & \nxt \ev{L}{\rho}{\Kf{appL}{M, \rho, \kappa}} \\[1ex]
  \rt{f}{\Kf{appL}{M, \rho, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{M}{\rho}{\Kf{appR}{f, \kappa}} \\
  \rt{w}{\Kf{appR}{\cloV{\lam{x} N}{\rho}, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{N}{\ext{\rho}{x}{w}}{\kappa}
\end{array}
\]

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos2}$}{Clos2}}\label{sec:t-cek-clos2}

A closure evaluates the terms of its free variables left to right, as a
tuple, and its value keeps their values as the environment of its code.
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  v &::=& \closV{x_1 \ldots x_n\, y.\, N}{v_1 \ldots v_n} \mid \cdots \\
  \kappa &::=& \Kf{appL}{M, \rho, \kappa} \mid \Kf{appR}{v, \kappa}
    \mid \Kf{closK}{x_1 \ldots x_n\, y.\, N, \kappa} \mid \cdots
\end{array}
\]
\[
\begin{array}{@{}l@{\;\mapsto\;}l@{\quad}l@{}}
  \ev{(\clos{M_1 \ldots M_n}{x_1 \ldots x_n\, y} N)}{\rho}{\kappa} &
    \nxt \tnext(\epsilon;\, M_1 \ldots M_n;\, \rho;\, \Kf{closK}{\bar{x}\, y.\, N, \kappa}) \\
  \ev{(L\;M)}{\rho}{\kappa} & \nxt \ev{L}{\rho}{\Kf{appL}{M, \rho, \kappa}} \\[1ex]
  \rt{\tupV{\bar{v}}}{\Kf{closK}{\bar{x}\, y.\, N, \kappa}} & \nxt \rt{\closV{\bar{x}\, y.\, N}{\bar{v}}}{\kappa} \\
  \rt{f}{\Kf{appL}{M, \rho, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{M}{\rho}{\Kf{appR}{f, \kappa}} \\
  \rt{w}{\Kf{appR}{\closV{\bar{x}\, y.\, N}{\bar{v}}, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{N}{\{\bar{x} {\mapsto} \bar{v}, y {\mapsto} w\}}{\kappa}
\end{array}
\]

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos3}$}{Clos3}}\label{sec:t-cek-clos3}

As $\Tl{Clos2}$, with environments typed by contexts: the code of a
closure is closed over its argument $y : \TAny$ and its environment
$e : \Tflat{\key{tup}\;n}$, and runs with the tuple of the closure's values.
\[
\begin{array}{@{}l@{\;\mapsto\;}l@{\quad}l@{}}
  \ev{(\clos{M_1 \ldots M_n}{e\, y} N)}{\rho}{\kappa} &
    \nxt \tnext(\epsilon;\, M_1 \ldots M_n;\, \rho;\, \Kf{closK}{e\, y.\, N, \kappa}) \\
  \rt{\tupV{\bar{v}}}{\Kf{closK}{e\, y.\, N, \kappa}} & \nxt \rt{\closV{e\, y.\, N}{\bar{v}}}{\kappa} \\
  \rt{w}{\Kf{appR}{\closV{e\, y.\, N}{\bar{v}}, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{N}{\{e {\mapsto} \tupV{\bar{v}}, y {\mapsto} w\}}{\kappa}
\end{array}
\]
with application ($\key{appL}$) as in $\Tl{Clos2}$.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos4}$}{Clos4}}\label{sec:t-cek-clos4}

Code is a value of type $\Tcode{n}$; a closure, of type
$\Tflat{\key{fun}}$, packs code of type $\Tcode{n}$ with an environment
of type $\Tflat{\key{tup}\;n}$ (the type $\Tflat{\key{fun}}$ hides $n$).
$\Tcallc{L}{M}$ evaluates the closure and the argument and runs the
closure's code with its environment and the argument, with no check,
since the closure has type $\Tflat{\key{fun}}$. There are no pairs and no
three-operand application as in the untyped Clos4.
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  v, f, u &::=& \TcodeV{e\, y.\, N} \mid \Tpack{v}{u} \mid \cdots \\
  \kappa &::=& \Kf{callL}{M, \rho, \kappa} \mid \Kf{callR}{f, \kappa}
    \mid \Kf{packL}{M, \rho, \kappa} \mid \Kf{packR}{v, \kappa} \mid \cdots
\end{array}
\]
\[
\begin{array}{@{}l@{\;\mapsto\;}l@{\quad}l@{}}
  \ev{\TcodeV{e\, y.\, N}}{\rho}{\kappa} & \nxt \rt{\TcodeV{e\, y.\, N}}{\kappa} \\
  \ev{\Tpack{L}{M}}{\rho}{\kappa} & \nxt \ev{L}{\rho}{\Kf{packL}{M, \rho, \kappa}} \\
  \ev{\Tcallc{L}{M}}{\rho}{\kappa} & \nxt \ev{L}{\rho}{\Kf{callL}{M, \rho, \kappa}} \\[1ex]
  \rt{v}{\Kf{packL}{M, \rho, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{M}{\rho}{\Kf{packR}{v, \kappa}} \\
  \rt{u}{\Kf{packR}{v, \kappa}} & \nxt \rt{\Tpack{v}{u}}{\kappa} \\
  \rt{f}{\Kf{callL}{M, \rho, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{M}{\rho}{\Kf{callR}{f, \kappa}} \\
  \rt{w}{\Kf{callR}{\Tpack{\TcodeV{e\, y.\, N}}{u}, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{N}{\{e {\mapsto} u, y {\mapsto} w\}}{\kappa}
\end{array}
\]

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos5}$ and $\Tl{Mon}$}{Clos5 and Mon}}\label{sec:t-cek-clos5}

A program runs with its table of definitions $d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1}$; values,
environments and continuations are indexed by the table. Code is a
reference $\codeV{j}$ to a definition, a typed index into the table, so
a call always finds its definition. The continuations are those of
$\Tl{Clos4}$, and $\key{let}$ may bind a variable of any type. The
machine takes values apart with total functions (the integer of a
value of type $\Tflat{\key{int}}$, the components of a tuple, the
payload of an injection), since their types say what they are; these
and the values are shared with the machines of $\Tl{Imp}$ and $\Tl{CFG}$.
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  v, f, u &::=& \codeV{j} \mid \Tpack{v}{u} \mid \cdots \\
  \Mach{\Tl{Clos5}}(d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M) &=& (\mathit{State}^{\Tl{Clos5}},\; \step{\Tl{Clos5}}_{d_0 \ldots d_{m-1}},\; \ev{M}{\emptyset}{\halt})
\end{array}
\]
The transitions are those of $\Tl{Clos4}$, with
\[
\begin{array}{@{}l@{\;\mapsto\;}l@{\quad}l@{}}
  \ev{\funref{j}}{\rho}{\kappa} & \nxt \rt{\codeV{j}}{\kappa} \\
  \rt{w}{\Kf{callR}{\Tpack{\codeV{j}}{u}, \kappa}} & \nxt \ev{N}{\{e {\mapsto} u, y {\mapsto} w\}}{\kappa}
    & d_j = \key{fun}\; e\,y.\,N
\end{array}
\]
in place of those for code. $\Tl{Mon}$ has no machine of its own: a
$\Tl{Mon}$ program $P$ runs as its embedding $\Tembed{P}$ into $\Tl{Clos5}$,
$\Mach{\Tl{Mon}}(P) = \Mach{\Tl{Clos5}}(\Tembed{P})$, which maps an atom to
the variable, literal or $\funref{j}$ it is and every other construct
to the $\Tl{Clos5}$ construct of the same name.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Imp}$}{Imp}}\label{sec:t-cek-imp}

An expression runs in a frame $\sigma$ of slots typed by its frame
context $\Delta$, the types of the slots assigned so far; a slot is a
typed index into $\Delta$, so an atom always has a value. The frame
grows: $\assign{x}{e_1}{e_2}$ and a $\key{case}$ assign the next slot,
written $\sigma, x {\mapsto} v$, which only the expression in their
scope sees, and a continuation keeps the frame it resumes in.
There is therefore no $\key{ret}$ state, no frames $F$ of operands, no
$\key{caseK}$ and no $\key{callK}$, as there are in Part~II's Imp machine.
The values are those of $\Tl{Clos5}$.
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  \mathit{State}^{\Tl{Imp}} &::=& \evi{e}{\sigma}{\kappa} \\
  \kappa &::=& \halt \mid \Kf{assignK}{x.e, \sigma, \kappa} \\
  \mathit{Res} &::=& \key{value}(v) \mid \key{fail} \mid \key{call}(f, w)
\end{array}
\]
A simple expression takes one step: its operands are atoms, so it gives
a value, fails, or calls a closure. $\mathit{run}$ is shared with $\Tl{CFG}$.
\[
\begin{array}{@{}l@{\;=\;}l@{\qquad}l@{\;=\;}l@{}}
  \sigma(c) & c & \sigma(\funref{j}) & \codeV{j} \\
  \mathit{run}(a, \sigma) & \key{value}(\sigma(a)) &
  \mathit{run}(\Tpack{a}{b}, \sigma) & \key{value}(\Tpack{\sigma(a)}{\sigma(b)}) \\
  \mathit{run}(\Tcallc{a}{b}, \sigma) & \key{call}(\sigma(a), \sigma(b)) &
  \mathit{run}(\tuple{a_1, \ldots, a_l}, \sigma) & \key{value}(\tupV{\sigma(a_1), \ldots, \sigma(a_l)}) \\
  \mathit{run}(\prj{i} a, \sigma) & \key{value}(v_i) \quad (\sigma(a) = \tupV{\bar{v}}) &
  \mathit{run}(\inl a, \sigma) & \key{value}(\inlV \sigma(a)) \\
  \mathit{run}(\inr a, \sigma) & \key{value}(\inrV \sigma(a)) &
  \mathit{run}(\prim{p}{a_1}{a_2}, \sigma) & \key{value}(\Tres{\delta(p, \sigma(a_1), \sigma(a_2))}) \\
  \mathit{run}(\Tinject{G} a, \sigma) & \key{value}(\TdynV{G}{\sigma(a)})
\end{array}
\]
\[
  \mathit{run}(\Tproject{t} a, \sigma) =
    \begin{cases} \key{value}(v) & \sigma(a) = \TdynV{H}{v},\; H = \Tflatof{t} \\ \key{fail} & \text{otherwise} \end{cases}
\]
A call runs the definition in a frame of its environment (slot~0) and
argument (slot~1), written $(u\, w)$, with the caller's continuation,
which holds the caller's frame. For a program with definitions
$d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1}$, each an expression over the slots $e$ and $y$:
\begin{align*}
  \mathit{cont}(v, \halt) &= \stp \obs(v) \\
  \mathit{cont}(v, \Kf{assignK}{x.e, \sigma, \kappa}) &= \nxt \evi{e}{(\sigma, x {\mapsto} v)}{\kappa} \\[1ex]
  \mathit{res}(\key{value}(v), \kappa) &= \mathit{cont}(v, \kappa) \\
  \mathit{res}(\key{fail}, \kappa) &= \stp \error \\
  \mathit{res}(\key{call}(\Tpack{\codeV{j}}{u}, w), \kappa) &= \nxt \evi{d_j}{(u\, w)}{\kappa} \\[1ex]
  \Mach{\Tl{Imp}}(d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; e) &= (\mathit{State}^{\Tl{Imp}},\; \step{\Tl{Imp}}_{d_0 \ldots d_{m-1}},\; \evi{e}{\emptyset}{\halt})
\end{align*}
\[
\begin{array}{@{}l@{\;\mapsto\;}l@{\quad}l@{}}
  \evi{r}{\sigma}{\kappa} & \mathit{res}(\mathit{run}(r, \sigma), \kappa) \\
  \evi{(\assign{x}{e_1}{e_2})}{\sigma}{\kappa} & \nxt \evi{e_1}{\sigma}{\Kf{assignK}{x.e_2, \sigma, \kappa}} \\
  \evi{(\Case{a}{x}{e_1}{x}{e_2})}{\sigma}{\kappa} & \nxt \evi{e_1}{(\sigma, x {\mapsto} u)}{\kappa} & \sigma(a) = \inlV u \\
  & \nxt \evi{e_2}{(\sigma, x {\mapsto} u)}{\kappa} & \sigma(a) = \inrV u
\end{array}
\]
where $r$ is a simple expression.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{CFG}$}{CFG}}\label{sec:t-cek-cfg}

The values, frames, $\mathit{Res}$ and $\mathit{run}$ are those of
$\Tl{Imp}$. A block is a tail typed by the frame context it starts in
and by the frame contexts of the blocks it may jump to; a label is a
typed index into those, so a jump always finds its block, and a block
reads only slots that are assigned. As in Part~II, a state runs a tail
with the blocks it may jump to, those of its body for the start of a
body and those older than block $l$ for block $l$, and
$\key{block}(\vec{t}, l) = (t_l;\; t_0 \ldots t_{l-1})$ is that of
Part~II's CFG machine, now total. An assignment or $\key{case}$ with
scope $\Delta'$ (a prefix of the frame context) forgets the slots after
$\Delta'$, which belong to a finished scope, and assigns the next one;
$\sigma|_{\Delta'}$ is $\sigma$ without them. A step runs one statement.
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  \mathit{State}^{\Tl{CFG}} &::=& \exc{t}{\sigma}{\vec{t}}{\kappa} \\
  t &::=& \key{return}\; r \mid x \mathrel{:=_{\Delta'}} r;\; t \mid \key{goto}\; l
    \mid \key{case}_{\Delta'}\;a\;\key{of}\;\key{inl}\,x \Rightarrow \key{goto}\;l_1 \mid \key{inr}\,x \Rightarrow \key{goto}\;l_2 \\
  \kappa &::=& \halt \mid \Kf{seqK}{\Delta', \sigma, x.t, \vec{t}, \kappa} \mid \Kf{retK}{\kappa}
\end{array}
\]
We abbreviate the $\key{case}$ tail to $\key{case}_{\Delta'}\;a\;(x.l_1)\;(x.l_2)$.
A call runs the start of the callee's body under $\key{seqK}$ (the
caller's assignment, which assigns the result and continues) or
$\key{retK}$ (the caller's $\key{return}$, which returns the result). For
a program with bodies $b_0, \ldots, b_{m-1}$:
\begin{align*}
  \mathit{enter}(\Tpack{\codeV{j}}{u}, w, \kappa) &= \nxt \exc{t}{(u\, w)}{\vec{t}}{\kappa}
    \qquad (b_j = (\vec{t};\; t)) \\[1ex]
  \mathit{cont}(v, \halt) &= \stp \obs(v) \\
  \mathit{cont}(v, \Kf{seqK}{\Delta', \sigma, x.t, \vec{t}, \kappa}) &= \nxt \exc{t}{(\sigma|_{\Delta'}, x {\mapsto} v)}{\vec{t}}{\kappa} \\
  \mathit{cont}(v, \Kf{retK}{\kappa}) &= \mathit{cont}(v, \kappa) \\[1ex]
  \Mach{\Tl{CFG}}(b_0, \ldots, b_{m-1};\; (\vec{t};\; t)) &=
    (\mathit{State}^{\Tl{CFG}},\; \step{\Tl{CFG}}_{b_0 \ldots b_{m-1}},\; \exc{t}{\emptyset}{\vec{t}}{\halt})
\end{align*}
\[
\begin{array}{@{}l@{\;\mapsto\;}l@{\quad}l@{}}
  \exc{(\key{return}\; r)}{\sigma}{\vec{t}}{\kappa} & \mathit{cont}(v, \kappa) & \mathit{run}(r, \sigma) = \key{value}(v) \\
  & \mathit{enter}(f, w, \Kf{retK}{\kappa}) & \mathit{run}(r, \sigma) = \key{call}(f, w) \\
  & \stp \error & \mathit{run}(r, \sigma) = \key{fail} \\
  \exc{(x \mathrel{:=_{\Delta'}} r;\; t)}{\sigma}{\vec{t}}{\kappa} &
    \nxt \exc{t}{(\sigma|_{\Delta'}, x {\mapsto} v)}{\vec{t}}{\kappa} & \mathit{run}(r, \sigma) = \key{value}(v) \\
  & \mathit{enter}(f, w, \Kf{seqK}{\Delta', \sigma, x.t, \vec{t}, \kappa}) & \mathit{run}(r, \sigma) = \key{call}(f, w) \\
  & \stp \error & \mathit{run}(r, \sigma) = \key{fail} \\
  \exc{(\key{goto}\; l)}{\sigma}{\vec{t}}{\kappa} & \nxt \exc{t'}{\sigma}{\vec{t}'}{\kappa} & (t';\; \vec{t}') = \key{block}(\vec{t}, l) \\
  \exc{(\key{case}_{\Delta'}\;a\;(x.l_1)\;(x.l_2))}{\sigma}{\vec{t}}{\kappa} &
    \nxt \exc{t'}{(\sigma|_{\Delta'}, x {\mapsto} u)}{\vec{t}'}{\kappa} & \sigma(a) = \inlV u,\; (t';\; \vec{t}') = \key{block}(\vec{t}, l_1) \\
  & \nxt \exc{t'}{(\sigma|_{\Delta'}, x {\mapsto} u)}{\vec{t}'}{\kappa} & \sigma(a) = \inrV u,\; (t';\; \vec{t}') = \key{block}(\vec{t}, l_2)
\end{array}
\]
Unlike Part~II's CFG machine, there is no state that
evaluates an operand or returns a value: a step runs a whole statement.

%-------------------------------------------------------------------------------
\section{Clocked Interpreters}\label{sec:t-interp}

The clocked interpreters of the typed languages
(\texttt{agda/Semantics/Interp/Typed}) use the results, programs and
clocks of Part~II (its section Results and Clocks): a clocked program $(\obs, \lambda k.\, \ldots)$, results
$\tmo$, $\error$ and $\Ret v$, and $\bind$. As there, the clock
decreases only when a function is called. Values and environments are
typed, so each interpreter is a total function of typed terms that
errs only where a project fails; the $\error$ cases of Part~II's
interpreters (an operator that is not a closure, $\key{get}$, $\key{branch}$
and $\key{prim}$ on values of the wrong kind, a missing definition or
block, $\lift{\bot}$) are gone.

\subsection{Values}\label{sec:t-interp-values}

The values (\texttt{Values.agda}) are shared by all the interpreters and
parameterized by what the code of a closure with an environment of
length $n$ is in each language, $C_n$. A closure, of type
$\Tflat{\key{fun}}$, is always code packed with the tuple of its
environment, even in the languages before $\Tl{Clos4}$, where code is
not a value of the language:
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  v, f, u &::=& \TcodeV{c} \mid \Tpack{\TcodeV{c}}{u} \mid c \mid \tupV{v_1, \ldots, v_n}
    \mid \inlV v \mid \inrV v \mid \TdynV{G}{v} \qquad (c \in C_n)
\end{array}
\]
\[
\begin{array}{l|ccccc}
  & \Tl{ISWIM} & \Tl{Clos2} & \Tl{Clos3} & \Tl{Clos4} & \Tl{Clos5},\ \Tl{Imp},\ \Tl{CFG} \\ \hline
  C_n & \lam{x} N & x_1 \ldots x_n\, y.\, N & e\, y.\, N & e\, y.\, N & \text{a definition } j \text{ (written } \codeV{j})
\end{array}
\]
$\obs$ is that of the machines (Section~\ref{sec:t-cek-shared}), and
\begin{align*}
  \key{project?}_t(\TdynV{H}{v}) &= \begin{cases} \Ret v & H = \Tflatof{t} \\ \error & \text{otherwise} \end{cases}
\end{align*}
is the only operation that can fail; the others are total:
$\key{nth}_i(\tupV{\bar{v}}) = v_i$, $\delta(p, n, m)$ gives
$\Tres{\delta(p, n, m)}$, and $\key{either}$ passes the payload of an
injection to one of two functions.

\subsection{Clauses common to all interpreters}

For $\ell$ one of $\Tl{ISWIM}$ to $\Tl{Clos5}$ (with $\EV{\ell}{k}$ for
$\Tl{Clos5}$ running with the table of definitions, as in Part~II):
\begin{align*}
  \EV{\ell}{k} &: \mathit{Env}(\Gamma) \to \mathit{Term}(\Gamma, A) \to \mathit{Result}(\mathit{Val}(A)) \\
  \Interp{\ell}(M) &= (\obs,\; \lambda k.\, \EV{\ell}{k}\,\emptyset\,M) \\[1ex]
  \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,x &= \Ret \rho(x) \\
  \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,c &= \Ret c \\
  \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,\tuple{M_1, \ldots, M_n} &=
    \Do \bar{v} \leftarrow \EVS{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,(M_1 \ldots M_n);\; \Ret \tupV{\bar{v}} \\
  \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,(\prj{i} M) &= \Do v \leftarrow \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,M;\; \Ret \key{nth}_i(v) \\
  \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,(\inl M) &= \Do v \leftarrow \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,M;\; \Ret (\inlV v)
    \qquad \text{and so for } \key{inr} \\
  \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,(\Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N}) &=
    \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,L \bind \lambda u.\, \key{either}(u,\; \lambda v.\, \EV{\ell}{k}\,\ext{\rho}{x}{v}\,M,\\
  &\qquad \lambda v.\, \EV{\ell}{k}\,\ext{\rho}{y}{v}\,N) \\
  \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,\prim{p}{L}{M} &=
    \Do v \leftarrow \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,L;\; w \leftarrow \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,M;\; \Ret \delta(p, v, w) \\
  \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,(\Let x = M \In N) &=
    \Do v \leftarrow \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,M;\; \EV{\ell}{k}\,\ext{\rho}{x}{v}\,N \\
  \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,(\Tinject{G} M) &= \Do v \leftarrow \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,M;\; \Ret \TdynV{G}{v} \\
  \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,(\Tproject{t} M) &= \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,M \bind \key{project?}_t \\[1ex]
  \CALL{\ell}{0}\,f\,w &= \tmo
\end{align*}
with $\EVS{\ell}{k}$ as in Part~II. An application evaluates its operator
and its operand and then calls, $\Do f \leftarrow \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,L;\;
w \leftarrow \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,M;\; \CALL{\ell}{k}\,f\,w$, for $L\;M$ in
$\Tl{ISWIM}$ to $\Tl{Clos3}$ and for $\Tcallc{L}{M}$ in $\Tl{Clos4}$ and
$\Tl{Clos5}$. The call never errs, since $f$ is a closure by its type.

\subsection{Closures and calls}

\begin{align*}
  \EV{\Tl{ISWIM}}{k}\,\rho\,(\lam{x} N) &= \Ret \Tpack{\TcodeV{\lam{x} N}}{\tupV{\rho}} \\
  \CALL{\Tl{ISWIM}}{k+1}\,\Tpack{\TcodeV{\lam{x} N}}{\tupV{\rho}}\,w &= \EV{\Tl{ISWIM}}{k}\,\ext{\rho}{x}{w}\,N \\[1ex]
  \EV{\Tl{Clos2}}{k}\,\rho\,(\clos{M_1 \ldots M_n}{\bar{x}\, y} N) &=
    \Do \bar{v} \leftarrow \EVS{\Tl{Clos2}}{k}\,\rho\,(M_1 \ldots M_n);\; \Ret \Tpack{\TcodeV{\bar{x}\, y.\, N}}{\tupV{\bar{v}}} \\
  \CALL{\Tl{Clos2}}{k+1}\,\Tpack{\TcodeV{\bar{x}\, y.\, N}}{\tupV{\bar{v}}}\,w &=
    \EV{\Tl{Clos2}}{k}\,\{\bar{x} {\mapsto} \bar{v}, y {\mapsto} w\}\,N \\[1ex]
  \EV{\Tl{Clos3}}{k}\,\rho\,(\clos{M_1 \ldots M_n}{e\, y} N) &=
    \Do \bar{v} \leftarrow \EVS{\Tl{Clos3}}{k}\,\rho\,(M_1 \ldots M_n);\; \Ret \Tpack{\TcodeV{e\, y.\, N}}{\tupV{\bar{v}}} \\
  \CALL{\ell}{k+1}\,\Tpack{\TcodeV{e\, y.\, N}}{u}\,w &=
    \EV{\ell}{k}\,\{e {\mapsto} u, y {\mapsto} w\}\,N \qquad (\ell \in \{\Tl{Clos3}, \Tl{Clos4}\}) \\[1ex]
  \EV{\Tl{Clos4}}{k}\,\rho\,\TcodeV{e\, y.\, N} &= \Ret \TcodeV{e\, y.\, N} \\
  \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,\Tpack{L}{M} &=
    \Do c \leftarrow \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,L;\; u \leftarrow \EV{\ell}{k}\,\rho\,M;\; \Ret \Tpack{c}{u} \\
  &\qquad (\ell \in \{\Tl{Clos4}, \Tl{Clos5}\}) \\[1ex]
  \EV{\Tl{Clos5}}{k}\,\rho\,\funref{j} &= \Ret \codeV{j} \\
  \CALL{\Tl{Clos5}}{k+1}\,\Tpack{\codeV{j}}{u}\,w &=
    \EV{\Tl{Clos5}}{k}\,\{e {\mapsto} u, y {\mapsto} w\}\,N
\end{align*}
where $d_j = \key{fun}\; e\,y.\,N$.
A reference to a missing definition cannot be written, so the
$\error$ case of Part~II's $\CALL{\vec{d}}{k}$ is gone. $\Tl{Mon}$ has no
interpreter of its own: $\Interp{\Tl{Mon}}(P) = \Interp{\Tl{Clos5}}(\Tembed{P})$.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Imp}$}{Imp}}

An expression runs in a frame $\sigma$ typed by its frame context, the
values of the slots assigned so far, and returns a value; unlike Part~II,
it does not return the frame, since an assignment extends the frame only
for its scope. An atom is read from the frame and cannot fail. The value
of a simple expression, given how to call a closure ($C$), is shared
with $\Tl{CFG}$ (the Agda's $\key{eval\text{-}simple}$):
\begin{align*}
  \key{simple}_C(\sigma, a) &= \Ret \sigma(a) \qquad
  \key{simple}_C(\sigma, \Tpack{a}{b}) = \Ret \Tpack{\sigma(a)}{\sigma(b)} \\
  \key{simple}_C(\sigma, \Tcallc{a}{b}) &= C(\sigma(a), \sigma(b)) \\
  \key{simple}_C(\sigma, \tuple{a_1, \ldots, a_l}) &= \Ret \tupV{\sigma(a_1), \ldots, \sigma(a_l)} \qquad
  \key{simple}_C(\sigma, \prj{i} a) = \Ret \key{nth}_i(\sigma(a)) \\
  \key{simple}_C(\sigma, \inl a) &= \Ret (\inlV \sigma(a)) \qquad
  \key{simple}_C(\sigma, \inr a) = \Ret (\inrV \sigma(a)) \\
  \key{simple}_C(\sigma, \prim{p}{a_1}{a_2}) &= \Ret \delta(p, \sigma(a_1), \sigma(a_2)) \\
  \key{simple}_C(\sigma, \Tinject{G} a) &= \Ret \TdynV{G}{\sigma(a)} \qquad
  \key{simple}_C(\sigma, \Tproject{t} a) = \key{project?}_t(\sigma(a))
\end{align*}
For a program with definitions $\vec{d} = d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1}$:
\begin{align*}
  \Interp{\Tl{Imp}}(\vec{d};\; e) &= (\obs,\; \lambda k.\, \EV{\vec{d}}{k}\,\emptyset\,e) \\[1ex]
  \EV{\vec{d}}{k}\,\sigma\,r &= \key{simple}_{\CALL{\vec{d}}{k}}(\sigma, r) \\
  \EV{\vec{d}}{k}\,\sigma\,(\assign{x}{e_1}{e_2}) &=
    \Do v \leftarrow \EV{\vec{d}}{k}\,\sigma\,e_1;\; \EV{\vec{d}}{k}\,(\sigma, x {\mapsto} v)\,e_2 \\
  \EV{\vec{d}}{k}\,\sigma\,(\Case{a}{x}{e_1}{x}{e_2}) &=
    \key{either}(\sigma(a),\; \lambda v.\, \EV{\vec{d}}{k}\,(\sigma, x {\mapsto} v)\,e_1,\;
      \lambda v.\, \EV{\vec{d}}{k}\,(\sigma, x {\mapsto} v)\,e_2) \\[1ex]
  \CALL{\vec{d}}{0}\,f\,w &= \tmo \\
  \CALL{\vec{d}}{k+1}\,\Tpack{\codeV{j}}{u}\,w &= \EV{\vec{d}}{k}\,(u\, w)\,d_j
\end{align*}
where $(u\, w)$ is the frame of the environment (slot~0) and the argument (slot~1).

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{CFG}$}{CFG}}

A tail runs in a frame with the blocks it may jump to, and returns a
value; $\key{simple}$ is that of $\Tl{Imp}$ and $\sigma|_{\Delta'}$ that
of the machine. Only calls tick; a jump does not, and runs its block
with the blocks older than it, so the interpreter is structurally
recursive on the clock, the blocks and the tail. A label always names a
block, so $\key{jump}$ cannot fail. For a program with bodies
$\vec{b} = b_0, \ldots, b_{m-1}$:
\begin{align*}
  \Interp{\Tl{CFG}}(\vec{b};\; (\vec{t};\; t)) &= (\obs,\; \lambda k.\, \key{tail}^k_{\vec{b}}\,\vec{t}\,\emptyset\,t) \\[1ex]
  \key{tail}^k_{\vec{b}}\,\vec{t}\,\sigma\,(\key{return}\; r) &= \key{simple}_{\CALL{\vec{b}}{k}}(\sigma, r) \\
  \key{tail}^k_{\vec{b}}\,\vec{t}\,\sigma\,(x \mathrel{:=_{\Delta'}} r;\; t) &=
    \Do v \leftarrow \key{simple}_{\CALL{\vec{b}}{k}}(\sigma, r);\;
    \key{tail}^k_{\vec{b}}\,\vec{t}\,(\sigma|_{\Delta'}, x {\mapsto} v)\,t \\
  \key{tail}^k_{\vec{b}}\,\vec{t}\,\sigma\,(\key{goto}\; l) &= \key{jump}^k_{\vec{b}}\,\vec{t}\,l\,\sigma \\
  \key{tail}^k_{\vec{b}}\,\vec{t}\,\sigma\,(\key{case}_{\Delta'}\;a\;(x.l_1)\;(x.l_2)) &=
    \key{either}(\sigma(a),\; \lambda u.\, \key{jump}^k_{\vec{b}}\,\vec{t}\,l_1\,(\sigma|_{\Delta'}, x {\mapsto} u),\;
      \lambda u.\, \key{jump}^k_{\vec{b}}\,\vec{t}\,l_2\,(\sigma|_{\Delta'}, x {\mapsto} u)) \\[1ex]
  \key{jump}^k_{\vec{b}}\,\vec{t}\,l\,\sigma &= \key{tail}^k_{\vec{b}}\,(t_0, \ldots, t_{l-1})\,\sigma\,t_l
    \qquad (\vec{t} = t_0, t_1, \ldots) \\[1ex]
  \CALL{\vec{b}}{0}\,f\,w &= \tmo \\
  \CALL{\vec{b}}{k+1}\,\Tpack{\codeV{j}}{u}\,w &= \key{tail}^k_{\vec{b}}\,\vec{t}\,(u\, w)\,t
    \qquad (b_j = (\vec{t};\; t))
\end{align*}

\subsection{The value relation}

The proofs of the passes between typed languages
(\texttt{agda/proof/Semantics/Interp/Typed/Rel.agda}) relate the values
of two interpreters by $v \approx w$ when they have the same shape, given a relation on
their codes and one on closures (for most passes, related codes with the
same environment; for optimize, the target's environment may drop
some of the source's values); related values are observed alike, and
the operations above ($\delta$, $\key{nth}$, $\key{either}$,
$\key{project?}$) give related results.


\section{Reduction Semantics}\label{sec:t-red}

The typed languages with binders, $\Tl{ISWIM}$ and $\Tl{Clos2}$--$\Tl{Clos5}$
(Section~\ref{sec:t-langs}), get a reduction semantics by substitution, as
the untyped ones do in Part~II (Section~\ref{sec:red}): a state is a closed
term, and a step reduces a redex in an evaluation context, substituting
values for bound variables
(\texttt{Semantics/\allowbreak Reduction/\allowbreak Typed/\allowbreak
\{ISWIM,\allowbreak Clos2,\allowbreak Clos3,\allowbreak Clos4,\allowbreak Clos5\}.agda}).
The framework is the same: a reduction says of a term whether it is a value
($\key{val}$), is stuck ($\error$), or reduces ($\key{to}\;M$); it is one
function $\mathit{red}$ written with the congruence
$\key{step\text{-}in}(f, \mathit{red}(X), k)$, and a closed program $M$ runs
as the machine $\mathcal{R}(M)$ of Section~\ref{sec:red}
(\texttt{Framework/Reduction.agda}). The operands of a tuple, and the free
variables of a closure, are reduced left to right by one combinator
(\texttt{Framework/ReductionVec.agda}),
\[
  \mathit{red}(M_1 \ldots M_n) =
  \key{step\text{-}in}(\square\, M_2 \ldots M_n,\; \mathit{red}(M_1),\;
  \key{step\text{-}in}(M_1\, \square,\; \mathit{red}(M_2 \ldots M_n),\; \key{val})) .
\]

What the types change:
\begin{itemize}
\item $\mathit{red}$ is defined on closed terms of every type $A$, and takes a
  term of type $A$ to one of type $A$, so every state is well typed by
  construction; a program has type $\TAny$.
\item There is no case for a variable (a closed term has none), and no case
  for an operand of the wrong shape: a value of type $\Tflat{\key{fun}}$ is
  a function, one of type $\Tflat{\key{tup}\,n}$ a tuple of $n$ values, of
  type $\Tflat{\key{sum}}$ an $\inl$ or an $\inr$, of type
  $\Tflat{\key{base}\,\key{Int}}$ an integer, and of type $\TAny$ an
  injection $\Tinject{G} V$. (The Agda still has a fall-through case to
  $\error$ in these redexes, so as not to unify types, but it is never
  reached.)
\item So the only error is a projection that finds a value injected from
  another flat type: $\Tproject{t} (\Tinject{G} V)$ with $G$ not the flat
  type that the tag $t$ tests.
\item Observations see through injections:
  $\obs(\Tinject{G} V) = \obs(V)$; otherwise $\obs$ is that of Part~II
  (a function, a closure, or a pack is observed as $\key{fun}$).
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Renaming and substitution.}
Terms are intrinsically scoped and typed: in $\Tl{ISWIM}$ and $\Tl{Clos2}$ by
the number of variables in scope (every variable has type $\TAny$), in
$\Tl{Clos3}$--$\Tl{Clos5}$ by a context of types, with typed variables.
Renaming and substitution are one traversal $\mathit{trav}_{\mathcal{K}}$,
parameterised by a \emph{kit} $\mathcal{K}$ (McBride's type-preserving
renaming and substitution; \texttt{Framework/Syntax/Kit.agda}): a kit says
what a variable is mapped to, how a variable is one, how one is a term, and
how one is moved under a binder. Renaming is the traversal with the kit of
variables, substitution the one with the kit of terms. On paper we write
$N[x := V]$ and $N[x_1 := V_1, \ldots, x_n := V_n]$, with named variables.

\paragraph{Values.}
The values are a predicate $\mathit{Value}$ in the proof halves
(\texttt{proof/\allowbreak Semantics/\allowbreak Reduction/\allowbreak Typed/\allowbreak *.agda}), given below for each
language, with the lemma that a value reduces to $\key{val}$, and (for
$\Tl{ISWIM}$, $\Tl{Clos2}$ and $\Tl{Clos3}$) that renaming and substitution
keep values. For $\Tl{ISWIM}$ and $\Tl{Clos5}$ the proof halves also give the
laws of the traversal: two traversals fuse into one, substitution by
variables is the identity, and so a substitution under a binder followed by
one for the bound variable is one substitution.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{ISWIM}$}{ISWIM-T}}

\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  V, W &::=& \lam{x} N \mid c \mid \tuple{V_1, \ldots, V_n} \mid \inl V \mid \inr V
    \mid \Tinject{G} V \\
  E &::=& \square \mid E\;M \mid V\;E \mid \tuple{V_1, \ldots, V_{i-1}, E, M_{i+1}, \ldots, M_n}
    \mid \prj{i} E \mid \inl E \mid \inr E \\
  &\mid& \Case{E}{x}{M}{y}{N} \mid \prim{p}{E}{M} \mid \prim{p}{V}{E}
    \mid \Let x = E \In N \mid \Tinject{G} E \mid \Tproject{t} E
\end{array}
\]
\[
\begin{array}{@{}r@{\;\to\;}l@{\quad}l@{}}
  (\lam{x} N)\;V & N[x := V] \\
  \prj{i} \tuple{V_1, \ldots, V_n} & V_i \\
  \Case{\inl V}{x}{M}{y}{N} & M[x := V] \\
  \Case{\inr V}{x}{M}{y}{N} & N[y := V] \\
  \Let x = V \In N & N[x := V] \\
  \key{sub}(n, m) & n - m \\
  \key{eq}(n, m) & \inl \Tunit & n = m \\
  \key{eq}(n, m) & \inr \Tunit & n \neq m \\
  \Tproject{t} (\Tinject{G} V) & V & G = \key{flat}(t) \\
  \Tproject{t} (\Tinject{G} V) & \error & G \neq \key{flat}(t)
\end{array}
\]
where $\key{flat}(t)$ is the flat type that the tag $t$ tests (a function, a
sum, an integer, or a tuple of at least one component), and $\Tunit$ is the
empty tuple injected at its type.
The differences from Part~II's ISWIM: the value $\Tinject{G} V$, and the
contexts and redexes of $\key{let}$, $\key{inject}$ and $\key{project}$; the
operator of an application is evaluated and then the argument, with no check
in between (the context is $V\;E$ for any value $V$, which has type
$\Tflat{\key{fun}}$ and so is a $\lambda$); $\prj{i}$ needs no arity check,
since $i < n$ is in its type; and the result of a primitive is a term of its
flat type ($\key{eq}$ gives $\inl \Tunit$ for true). An injection is a value
once its operand is, and is taken apart only by a projection.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos2}$ and $\Tl{Clos3}$}{Clos2-T and Clos3-T}}

As $\Tl{ISWIM}$, with closures in place of $\lambda$. A closure evaluates its
free variables left to right, and is a value once they are values. Its code
is closed, so no substitution enters it.
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  V &::=& \clos{V_1\ldots V_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N \mid c \mid \tuple{\bar{V}} \mid \inl V \mid \inr V
    \mid \Tinject{G} V \\
  E &::=& \clos{V_1 \ldots V_{i-1}\, E\, M_{i+1} \ldots M_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N \mid E\;M \mid V\;E \mid \cdots
\end{array}
\]
In $\Tl{Clos2}$ the code has a variable of type $\TAny$ for each free
variable and one for the argument:
\[
  (\clos{V_1\ldots V_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N)\;W \to N[x_1 := V_1, \ldots, x_n := V_n, y := W] .
\]
In $\Tl{Clos3}$ the code has two variables, $e$ of type
$\Tflat{\key{tup}\,n}$ for the tuple of the free variables and $y$ of type
$\TAny$ for the argument:
\[
  (\clos{V_1\ldots V_n}{e\, y} N)\;W \to N[e := \tuple{V_1, \ldots, V_n}, y := W] .
\]
The other contexts and redexes, $\key{inject}$ and $\key{project}$ included,
are those of $\Tl{ISWIM}$. These are the rules of Part~II's Clos2 and Clos3,
with injections and projections added and the shape checks gone.

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Clos4}$ and $\Tl{Clos5}$}{Clos4-T and Clos5-T}}

Here the typed languages differ most from Part~II. A closure is a pack of
closed code, of type $\Tcode{n}$, and an environment, of type
$\Tflat{\key{tup}\,n}$; the pack has type $\Tflat{\key{fun}}$, which hides
$n$. Code is a value, and so is a pack of values. A call
$\Tcallc{L}{M}$ has two operands, the closure and the argument (not the
three of Part~II's $V(U, W)$, and there is no $\key{pair}$, $\key{fst}$ or
$\key{snd}$): it evaluates both, left to right, and then calls the code of
the closure with its environment and the argument.
\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  V, U, W &::=& \Tcodetm{e}{y}{N} \mid \Tpack{V}{W} \mid c \mid \tuple{\bar{V}} \mid \inl V \mid \inr V
    \mid \Tinject{G} V \\
  E &::=& \Tpack{E}{M} \mid \Tpack{V}{E} \mid \Tcallc{E}{M} \mid \Tcallc{V}{E} \mid \cdots
\end{array}
\]
\[
  \Tcallc{\Tpack{\Tcodetm{e}{y}{N}}{U}}{W} \to N[e := U, y := W]
\]
The other contexts and redexes, $\key{let}$ included, are those of
$\Tl{ISWIM}$.

In $\Tl{Clos5}$ the code is global. A program
$(d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)$ has a table $\Sigma$, the lengths of the
environments of its definitions, and its terms are indexed by $\Sigma$ as
well as by their context: a reference $\funref{f}$, for $f$ an entry of
$\Sigma$ of length $n$, has type $\Tcode{n}$ and is a value (it replaces
$\Tcodetm{e}{y}{N}$ among the values). So a reference to a missing
definition cannot be written, where Part~II's Clos5 checks $k < m$ at the
call. The reduction $\mathit{red}_{\bar{d}}$ of a program with the
definitions $\bar{d}$ is that of $\Tl{Clos4}$, with
\[
  \Tcallc{\Tpack{\funref{f}}{U}}{W} \to N[e := U, y := W]
  \qquad \text{where } d_f = e\,y.\,N ,
\]
and the program runs as $\mathcal{R}(M)$ with $\mathit{red}_{\bar{d}}$. In
$\Tl{Clos5}$ a $\key{let}$ binds a value of any type, not only of type
$\TAny$ (remove complex operands binds operands of any type: code,
environments, and so on).

\subsection{\texorpdfstring{$\Tl{Mon}$, $\Tl{Imp}$, $\Tl{CFG}$ and X86}{Mon-T, Imp-T, CFG-T and X86}}

$\Tl{Mon}$ has no reduction semantics of its own: a $\Tl{Mon}$ program $P$
runs as its embedding $\ulcorner P \urcorner$ into $\Tl{Clos5}$
(\texttt{Language/Typed/Mon.agda}). So remove complex operands is proved by
a simulation of the $\Tl{Clos5}$ reduction by itself, and lift locals by a
simulation of the $\Tl{Clos5}$ reduction of the embedding by the $\Tl{Imp}$
machine. $\Tl{Imp}$, $\Tl{CFG}$ and X86 have no binders, so they keep their
machines: those of $\Tl{Imp}$ and $\Tl{CFG}$ are the typed CEK column's
(Section~\ref{sec:t-cek}), and X86's is Part~II's
(Section~\ref{sec:machine-x86}). The proofs of explicate control and select
instructions are the CEK column's.

\section{The Theorems}\label{sec:t-thm}

The public statements are in \texttt{agda/Theorems/}, each with its type
written out. Write $\mathit{Closed}(M)$ for an ISWIM program $M$ with no
free variables (the Agda's \texttt{Closed}, \texttt{Compiler/EncloseScope.agda}),
and
\[
  \pass{compile\text{-}x86} = \pass{select} \circ \pass{explicate} \circ
  \pass{lift} \circ \pass{rco} \circ \pass{globalize} \circ \pass{delay}
  \circ \pass{concretize}_\emptyset \circ \pass{optimize} \circ \pass{enclose} \circ
  \pass{cast\text{-}closed} \circ \pass{simplify}
\]
(Section~\ref{sec:t-passes}). Each pass is stated for every program of its
source language, except simplify and cast insertion, whose source is
untyped ISWIM. A $\Tl{Mon}$ program $P$ is given meaning, and run, as its
embedding $\Tembed{P}$ into $\Tl{Clos5}$: rco is stated as
$P \mapsto \Tembed{\pass{rco}(P)}$ and lift locals as
$\Tembed{P} \mapsto \pass{lift}(P)$. The whole compiler is stated for
each prefix, $\pass{compile}$ (into $\Tl{Clos5}$), $\pass{compile\text{-}mon}$
(through the embedding), $\pass{compile\text{-}imp}$,
$\pass{compile\text{-}cfg}$ and $\pass{compile\text{-}x86}$, for every
closed $M$.
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Denotational: graph model} (\texttt{Theorems/Denotational.agda};
  Section~\ref{sec:t-den}). Every pass but delay preserves the meaning
  exactly: $\SEM{M}\rho_0 \simeq \SEM{\pass{simplify}(M)}\rho_0$ for every
  $M$; $\SEM{M}\rho_0 \simeq \SEM{\pass{cast\text{-}closed}(M)}^{\Tl{ISWIM}}\rho_0$
  when $\mathit{Closed}(M)$; and likewise, with no precondition, for
  enclose, optimize, concretize, globalize, rco, lift locals, explicate
  control and select instructions (for select, for every $\Tl{CFG}$
  program: its frames are typed, so the frames of the X86 program are
  large enough by construction). Delay preserves and reflects the
  constants and nonemptiness: $c \in \SEM{M}^{\Tl{3}}\rho_0 \iff
  c \in \SEM{\pass{delay}(M)}^{\Tl{4}}\rho_0$, and likewise for
  $\NE$. So, when $\mathit{Closed}(M)$, the meaning of each prefix of the
  compiler applied to $M$ has the same constants as $\SEM{M}\rho_0$, and is
  nonempty exactly when $\SEM{M}\rho_0$ is (\texttt{compile-correct-const},
  \texttt{compile-correct-nonempty}, and so on).
\item \textbf{CEK machine + simulation} (\texttt{Theorems/Simulation.agda};
  Section~\ref{sec:t-cek}), \textbf{clocked interpreter + value relations}
  (\texttt{Theorems/Interpreter.agda}; Section~\ref{sec:t-interp}) and
  \textbf{reduction semantics + simulation} (\texttt{Theorems/Reduction.agda};
  Section~\ref{sec:t-red}). Each pass gives $\Equiv$ semantics: each
  terminates with an observation $o$ ($\error$ included) exactly when the
  other does, and one diverges exactly when the other does,
  \[
    (\forall o.\; \Term_{L_1}(o) \iff \Term_{L_2}(o)) \land (\Div_{L_1} \iff \Div_{L_2}),
  \]
  for simplify and cast insertion when $\mathit{Closed}(M)$, and for every
  program for the other passes. In the reduction column, the semantics of
  $\Tl{Imp}$, $\Tl{CFG}$ and X86 are the CEK machines. Each prefix of the
  compiler gives $\Equiv$ semantics with ISWIM when $\mathit{Closed}(M)$;
  and in particular, for $\pass{compile}$, the compiled program terminates
  with the same constants, terminates (with an answer) exactly when the
  original does, errs exactly when it does, and diverges exactly when it
  does (\texttt{compile-const}, \texttt{compile-converges},
  \texttt{compile-error}, \texttt{compile-diverges}).
\item \textbf{Adequacy} (\texttt{Theorems/Adequacy.agda}). For closed $M$:
  the graph model is sound and adequate for the ISWIM machine on constants
  ($c \in \SEM{M}\rho_0 \iff \Term_{\mathrm{ISWIM}(M)}(c)$) and on answers
  ($\SEM{M}\rho_0 \NE$ iff the machine terminates with an answer), and a
  program that errs or diverges means $\emptyset$; the same four hold for
  the $\Tl{Clos5}$ machine on $\pass{compile}(M)$, and the first two for
  the X86 machine on $\pass{compile\text{-}x86}(M)$. With the denotational
  theorems, observed through the machines, $\pass{compile}$ and
  $\pass{compile\text{-}x86}$ preserve the constants a program terminates
  with and whether it terminates with an answer.
\item \textbf{Graph model with errors} (\texttt{Theorems/GraphErrors.agda};
  Section~\ref{sec:t-gerr}). Every pass but delay and select preserves the
  meaning exactly, errors included (simplify and cast insertion when
  $\mathit{Closed}(M)$); delay preserves and reflects an error ($\omega$),
  the constants, and having a value (an element other than $\omega$);
  select is $\Same$ (an error, the same constants and a value together),
  and so is each prefix of the compiler, from $\SEM{M}\rho_0$, when
  $\mathit{Closed}(M)$. Adequacy: for closed $M$, the meaning tells apart
  the outcomes of the ISWIM machine on $M$, of the $\Tl{Clos5}$ machine on
  $\pass{compile}(M)$ and of the X86 machine on $\pass{compile\text{-}x86}(M)$:
  $\omega$ is in it iff the machine errs, $c$ iff it terminates with $c$,
  a value iff it terminates with an answer, and nothing iff it diverges.
  So the ISWIM machine on $M$ and the $\Tl{Clos5}$ and X86 machines on its
  compilation stop with an error together, stop with the same constants,
  stop with an answer together, and diverge together; and likewise for
  simplify, through the ISWIM machine. Adequacy for the X86 machine is that
  of Part~\ref{part:untyped}'s X86 machine, on the typed compiler's
  programs, which are well formed; for the $\Tl{Clos5}$ machine it is derived
  from the ISWIM machine's, $\Same$, and the CEK column's simulation, not
  proved by a logical relation of its own.
\end{itemize}

\part{The Untyped Pipeline (Preserved)}\label{part:untyped}

This part presents the untyped pipeline, the one that preceded the typed
pipeline of Part~\ref{part:typed}. It is preserved, and checked by
\texttt{make check}, in \texttt{agda/Untyped/} (module prefix
\texttt{Untyped.}), whose paths mirror those of \texttt{agda/}; it is no
longer the main development. Its intermediate languages are untyped, and
their semantics check the kinds of values themselves:
\[
\begin{array}{l}
\mathrm{ISWIM} \xrightarrow{\pass{simplify}} \mathrm{ISWIM}
  \xrightarrow{\pass{enclose}} \mathrm{Clos2} \xrightarrow{\pass{optimize}} \mathrm{Clos2}
  \xrightarrow{\pass{concretize}} \mathrm{Clos3} \xrightarrow{\pass{delay}} \mathrm{Clos4}
  \xrightarrow{\pass{globalize}} \mathrm{Clos5} \\
\qquad \xrightarrow{\pass{rco}} \text{monadic Clos5}
  \xrightarrow{\pass{lift}} \mathrm{Imp} \xrightarrow{\pass{explicate}} \mathrm{CFG}
  \xrightarrow{\pass{select}} \mathrm{X86}
\end{array}
\]
Its definitions of ISWIM, simplify and X86, and the semantics of ISWIM
and X86 in each style, are shared with the typed pipeline; everything
else here is in \texttt{agda/Untyped/}. The part has the languages
(Section~\ref{sec:u-langs}) and the passes (Section~\ref{sec:u-passes}),
and then one section per column, each with its semantics and the
relations of its proofs: the graph model (Section~\ref{sec:u-den}), CEK
machines (Section~\ref{sec:u-cek}), clocked interpreters
(Section~\ref{sec:u-interp}), reduction semantics
(Section~\ref{sec:u-red}), and the graph model with errors
(Section~\ref{sec:graph-errors}). The ``Where it doesn't match the Agda
literally'' notes and the contents list in About this document are for
this part.

\section{Languages}\label{sec:u-langs}

\subsection{ISWIM}

The source language: a call-by-value $\lambda$-calculus with constants, tuples, sums, primitive operations $p$ on integers $n, m$, and $\key{let}$, which evaluates $M$ and then $N$ with $x$ bound to its value.
There are no booleans: $\key{eq}$ returns $\inl\tuple{}$ for true and $\inr\tuple{}$ for false, so $\key{case}$ serves as $\key{if}$.
\begin{syntax}
  & p &::=& \key{sub} \mid \key{eq} \\
  & L, M, N &::=& x \mid \lam{x} N \mid L\;M \mid c
    \mid \tuple{M_1,\ldots,M_n} \mid \prj{i} M \\
  &&& \mid \inl M \mid \inr M \mid \Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N} \mid \prim{p}{L}{M}
    \mid \Let x = M \In N
\end{syntax}
\[
  \FV(\lam{y} N) = \FV(N) \setminus \{y\}
  \qquad
  \delta(\key{sub}, n, m) = n - m
  \qquad
  \delta(\key{eq}, n, m) = (n = m)
\]

\subsection{Clos2}

Like ISWIM, but each $\lambda$ becomes a closure that binds the values of its free variables $z_1\ldots z_n$ to names $x_1\ldots x_n$, so its body refers only to those names and its parameter.
\begin{syntax}
  & L, M, N &::=& x \mid \clos{z_1\ldots z_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N \mid L\;M \mid c
    \mid \tuple{M_1,\ldots,M_n} \mid \prj{i} M \\
  &&& \mid \inl M \mid \inr M \mid \Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N} \mid \prim{p}{L}{M}
    \mid \Let x = M \In N
\end{syntax}
\[
  \FV(\clos{z_1\ldots z_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N) = \{z_1, \ldots, z_n\}
\]

\subsection{Clos3}

Each closure now packs references $r_1\ldots r_n$ to its free variables into one tuple bound to $e$, so its body reaches them by projections from $e$.
\begin{syntax}
  & r &::=& x \mid \prj{i} x \\
  & L, M, N &::=& x \mid \clos{r_1\ldots r_n}{e\, y} N \mid L\;M \mid c
    \mid \tuple{M_1,\ldots,M_n} \mid \prj{i} M \\
  &&& \mid \inl M \mid \inr M \mid \Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N} \mid \prim{p}{L}{M}
    \mid \Let x = M \In N
\end{syntax}
\[
  \FV(\clos{r_1\ldots r_n}{e\, y} N) = \FV(r_1) \cup \cdots \cup \FV(r_n)
\]

\subsection{Clos4}

Closures are explicit pairs of closed code $\fun{e\,y.\,N}$ and an environment, built with $\key{pair}$, taken apart with $\key{fst}$ and $\key{snd}$, and applied to an environment and an argument with $L(M, N)$; $\key{let}$, from the source, is also used by the delayed application.
\begin{syntax}
  & L, M, N &::=& x \mid \fun{e\,y.\,N} \mid L(M, N) \mid c
    \mid \pair{M}{N} \mid \FST M \mid \SND M \mid \prim{p}{L}{M} \\
  &&& \mid \tuple{M_1,\ldots,M_n} \mid \prj{i} M
    \mid \inl M \mid \inr M \mid \Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N}
    \mid \Let x = M \In N
\end{syntax}
\[
  \FV(\fun{e\,y.\,N}) = \emptyset
\]

\subsection{Clos5}

Like Clos4, but the code is hoisted into a program's list of global definitions $d_k$, which terms refer to as $\funref{k}$.
\begin{syntax}
  & L, M, N &::=& x \mid \funref{k} \mid L(M, N) \mid c
    \mid \pair{M}{N} \mid \FST M \mid \SND M \mid \prim{p}{L}{M} \\
  &&& \mid \tuple{M_1,\ldots,M_n} \mid \prj{i} M
    \mid \inl M \mid \inr M \mid \Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N}
    \mid \Let x = M \In N \\[1ex]
  & d &::=& \key{fun}\; e\,y.\,N \qquad\qquad (\FV(\key{fun}\; e\,y.\,N) = \emptyset) \\
  & P &::=& (d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)
\end{syntax}

\subsection{Monadic Clos5}

The sublanguage of Clos5 in monadic normal form: the operands of every construct but $\key{let}$ are atoms $a$, while the right-hand side and body of $\key{let}$ and the branches of $\key{case}$ may be complex.
\begin{syntax}
  & a &::=& x \mid \funref{k} \mid c \\
  & M, N &::=& a \mid a_1(a_2, a_3) \mid \pair{a_1}{a_2} \mid \FST a \mid \SND a
    \mid \prim{p}{a_1}{a_2} \\
  &&& \mid \tuple{a_1,\ldots,a_n} \mid \prj{i} a
    \mid \inl a \mid \inr a \mid \Case{a}{x}{M}{y}{N}
    \mid \Let x = M \In N \\[1ex]
  & d &::=& \key{fun}\; e\,y.\,N \\
  & P &::=& (d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)
\end{syntax}

\subsection{Imp}

An imperative language with structured control flow and no binders. A
body $(n; e)$ runs $e$ in a frame of $n$ local slots $s \in \{0, \ldots,
n-1\}$, and an atom names a slot of the current frame. The assignment
$\assign{s}{e_1}{e_2}$ runs $e_1$, stores its value in slot $s$, and runs
$e_2$; $\Casei{a}{s}{e_1}{e_2}$ stores the payload of the injection in slot
$s$ before running a branch. Assignments are seen by everything that
runs after them in the same frame. A call $a_1(a_2, a_3)$ runs the body
of the definition $a_1$ refers to in a fresh frame with $a_2$ (the free
variables) in slot $0$ and $a_3$ (the argument) in slot $1$; reading or
assigning a slot beyond the frame errors.
\begin{syntax}
  & a &::=& s \mid \funref{k} \mid c \\
  & r &::=& a \mid a_1(a_2, a_3) \mid \pair{a_1}{a_2} \mid \FST a \mid \SND a
    \mid \prim{p}{a_1}{a_2}
    \mid \tuple{a_1,\ldots,a_n} \mid \prj{i} a
    \mid \inl a \mid \inr a \\
  & e &::=& r \mid \Casei{a}{s}{e_1}{e_2}
    \mid \assign{s}{e_1}{e_2} \\[1ex]
  & b &::=& (n;\; e) \\
  & P &::=& (b_0, \ldots, b_{m-1};\; b)
\end{syntax}
The simple expressions $r$ are shared with CFG.

\subsection{CFG}

A C-like language: each body is a control-flow graph of blocks, tails
$t$ that end in $\key{return}$, a $\key{goto}$, or a $\key{case}$ that
assigns the payload to slot $s$ and jumps to block $l_1$ or $l_2$. Frames,
slots and calls are as in Imp, and a call runs the start $t$ of the
callee's body. The blocks $t_0, \ldots, t_{j-1}$ of a body are numbered
in order of creation; block $t_l$ may jump only to blocks $l' < l$, and
the start to any block, so control flow within a body is acyclic. A
jump to any other label errors.
\begin{syntax}
  & t &::=& \key{return}\; r \mid \assign{s}{r}{t} \mid \key{goto}\; l
    \mid \key{case}\;a\;\key{of}_{s}\;\key{goto}\;l_1 \mid \key{goto}\;l_2 \\[1ex]
  & b &::=& (n;\; t_0, \ldots, t_{j-1};\; t) \\
  & P &::=& (b_0, \ldots, b_{m-1};\; b)
\end{syntax}

\subsection{X86}

x86 with variables over the same values as before. The locations $d$ are
the slots of the frame and the registers; an operand $o$ is a location,
an immediate, or the label of a global definition. A flag is set by
$\key{cmpq}$ and $\key{testinl}$ and read by $\key{sete}$ and $\key{je}$.
The heap is abstract: $\key{alloc}$ builds a pair, tuple or injection
from its operands, and $\key{load}$ takes a field of one. $\key{callq}\;o$
reads $\%\key{rdi}$ and $\%\key{rsi}$ and runs the body of the definition
$o$ refers to, with them as they are, $\%\key{rax}$ unset and no flag, in
a fresh frame, and continues with the result in $\%\key{rax}$; it
clobbers $\%\key{rdi}$, $\%\key{rsi}$ and the flag. $\key{retq}$ reads
$\%\key{rax}$. Reading an unset register or flag errors. Frames,
blocks and jumps are as in CFG.
\begin{syntax}
  & d &::=& s \mid \%\key{rax} \mid \%\key{rdi} \mid \%\key{rsi} \\
  & o &::=& d \mid c \mid \funref{k} \\
  & i &::=& \key{movq}\; o, d \mid \key{subq}\; o, d \mid \key{cmpq}\; o_2, o_1
    \mid \key{sete}\; d \mid \key{testinl}\; o \\
  &&& \mid \key{alloc}\; \mathit{sh}\; \vec{o}, d \mid \key{load}\; f\; o, d
    \mid \key{callq}\; o \\
  & \mathit{sh} &::=& \key{pair} \mid \key{tuple} \mid \key{inl} \mid \key{inr}
  \qquad f \;::=\; \key{fst} \mid \key{snd} \mid \pi_i \mid \key{payload} \\
  & j &::=& \key{retq} \mid \key{jmp}\; l \mid \key{je}\; l_1, l_2 \\
  & \beta &::=& i_1; \ldots; i_p;\; j \\
  & b &::=& (n;\; \beta_0, \ldots, \beta_{q-1};\; \beta) \\
  & P &::=& (b_0, \ldots, b_{m-1};\; b)
\end{syntax}

%===============================================================================
\section{Compilation}\label{sec:u-passes}

\[
  \pass{compile} = \pass{globalize} \circ \pass{delay} \circ \pass{concretize}_{\mathit{id}}
     \circ \pass{optimize} \circ \pass{enclose} \circ \pass{simplify}
\]

\begin{align*}
  \pass{compile\text{-}mon} &= \pass{rco} \circ \pass{compile}
  &
  \pass{compile\text{-}imp} &= \pass{lift} \circ \pass{compile\text{-}mon} \\
  \pass{compile\text{-}cfg} &= \pass{explicate} \circ \pass{compile\text{-}imp}
  &
  \pass{compile\text{-}x86} &= \pass{select} \circ \pass{compile\text{-}cfg}
\end{align*}

In each pass, the clauses not shown are homomorphic.

\subsection{Simplify (ISWIM \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} ISWIM)}

Simplify, a simplified version of the source optimizer cp0 of Chez Scheme,
is the first pass of $\pass{compile}$. It carries what
it knows of each variable, $E(x) = (T, K)$: the term $T$ that replaces $x$ (a
variable or a small value), and what $x$ is known to be, $K$: a value, a
function $\langle \lam{y} N, E_f \rangle$ (its body before simplification, with
what was known where it was defined), or $\bot$. On a term it returns the
same, $\pass{simp}_E(M) = (M', K)$; we write $\pass{simp}_E(M)$ for $M'$ and
$\mathcal{K}_E(M)$ for $K$. A value is
\emph{small} when it is a variable, a constant, $\emptytup$, or $\inl a$ or
$\inr a$ of one of those; only small values are duplicated. Values are known
to be themselves, and $\mathcal{K}_E(\lam{y} N) = \langle \lam{y} N, E \rangle$;
under a binder that stays, its variable is itself, known only as itself
($E'$ below). A function body $N$ bound to an operand, $\pass{let}_E(x.N, M)$,
is shared by $\key{let}$, $\beta$ and inlining:
\begin{align*}
  \pass{let}_E(x.N, M) &= \pass{simp}_{\ext{E}{x}{(V, \mathcal{K}_E(M))}}(N)
    && \text{if } V = \pass{simp}_E(M) \text{ is small} \\
  &= \Let x = V \In \pass{simp}_{\ext{E}{x}{(x, \mathcal{K}_E(M))}}(N)
    && \text{if } V = \pass{simp}_E(M) \text{ is another value} \\
  &= \Let x = \pass{simp}_E(M) \In \pass{simp}_{E'}(N)
    && \text{otherwise} \\[1ex]
  \pass{simp}_E(x) &= E(x) \\
  \pass{simp}_E(\Let x = M \In N) &= \pass{let}_E(x.N, M) \\
  \pass{simp}_E((\lam{x} N)\;M) &= \pass{let}_E(x.N, M) \\
  \pass{simp}_E(L\;M) &= \pass{let}_{E_f}(x.N, M)
    && \text{if } \mathcal{K}_E(L) = \langle \lam{x} N, E_f \rangle \text{ and the result is small enough} \\
  \pass{simp}_E(\prim{p}{L}{M}) &= \res{\delta(p, n, m)}
    && \text{if } \mathcal{K}_E(L) = n,\; \mathcal{K}_E(M) = m \\
  \pass{simp}_E(\Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N}) &= \pass{simp}_{\ext{E}{x}{(K, K)}}(M)
    && \text{if } \mathcal{K}_E(L) = \inl K,\; K \text{ small} \\
  \pass{simp}_E(\prj{i} L) &= K_i
    && \text{if } \mathcal{K}_E(L) = \tuple{K_1, \ldots, K_n},\; K_i \text{ small} \\
  \mathcal{K}_E(\prj{i} L) &= K_i
    && \text{if } \mathcal{K}_E(L) = \tuple{K_1, \ldots, K_n} \\[1ex]
  \pass{simplify}(M) &= \pass{simp}_{x \mapsto (x, x)}(M)
\end{align*}
and likewise for $\inr$. Here $\res{o}$ is the term of a primitive's result
(a constant, or $\inl \emptytup$ or $\inr \emptytup$ for $\key{eq}$). The third
clause of $\pass{simp}$ is inlining: the known function's body is simplified
again, at the call, from the source, under $E_f$ and what is known of the
operand, and replaces the call if it has at most $24$ nodes; $\pass{simp}$
carries fuel ($4$ in $\pass{simplify}$), which inlining decreases, so that it
is structurally recursive. It drops only evaluations whose value is known,
which have no effect (the operator of an inlined call), and otherwise keeps
the order of evaluation: as in cp0, an operand that is not a value is never
substituted, but stays bound by a $\key{let}$, and is evaluated before the
body even if the body does not use it. Enclose keeps that $\key{let}$, so a
residual binding allocates no closure.

\begin{lemma}[Simplify Keeps Programs Closed]\label{lem:simplify-closed}
For every ISWIM term $M$,
\[
  \FV(M) = \emptyset \implies \FV(\pass{simplify}(M)) = \emptyset .
\]
\end{lemma}

\subsection{CC: Enclose (ISWIM \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} Clos2)}

Enclose decides which variables each closure captures (its free variables) and closes off its body, so that the body sees the captured values only through its own bound names.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{enclose}(\lam{y} N) &= \clos{x_1\ldots x_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} \pass{enclose}(N)
    \qquad\text{where } x_1, \ldots, x_n = \FV(\lam{y} N)
\end{align*}

\subsection{CC: Optimize (Clos2 \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} Clos2)}

Optimize removes the captured variables that the body does not use.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{optimize}(\clos{z_1\ldots z_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N) &=
    \clos{z_{i_1}\ldots z_{i_k}}{x_{i_1}\ldots x_{i_k}\, y} \pass{optimize}(N) \\
  \text{where } \{ i_1 < \cdots < i_k \} &= \{ i \mid x_i \in \FV(N) \}
\end{align*}

\subsection{CC: Concretize (Clos2 \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} Clos3)}

Concretize stores each closure's captured values in a single tuple and turns their uses in the body into projections from that tuple.
\[
  \varphi : \X \to \mathit{Ref}
  \qquad (\mathit{Ref} \text{ is the set of Clos3 references } r)
\]
\begin{align*}
  \pass{concretize}_\varphi(x) &= \varphi(x) \\
  \pass{concretize}_\varphi(\clos{z_1\ldots z_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N) &=
    \clos{\varphi(z_1)\ldots\varphi(z_n)}{e\,y}
       \pass{concretize}_{\varphi_n}(N) \\
  \text{where } \varphi_n &= \{ y \mapsto y,\; x_1 \mapsto \prj{1} e,\; \ldots,\; x_n \mapsto \prj{n} e \},
    \quad e \text{ fresh} \\
  \pass{concretize}_\varphi(\Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N}) &=
    \key{case}\;\pass{concretize}_\varphi(L)\;\key{of} \\
    &\qquad \key{inl}\,x \Rightarrow \pass{concretize}_{\ext{\varphi}{x}{x}}(M)
     \mid \key{inr}\,y \Rightarrow \pass{concretize}_{\ext{\varphi}{y}{y}}(N) \\
  \pass{concretize}_\varphi(\Let x = M \In N) &=
    \Let x = \pass{concretize}_\varphi(M) \In \pass{concretize}_{\ext{\varphi}{x}{x}}(N)
\end{align*}

\subsection{CC: Delay (Clos3 \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} Clos4)}

Delay represents each closure as an explicit pair of closed code and its environment tuple, and makes application pass the environment to the code.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{delay}(\clos{r_1\ldots r_n}{e\,y} N) &=
    \pair{\fun{e\,y.\,\pass{delay}(N)}}{\tuple{r_1,\ldots,r_n}} \\
  \pass{delay}(L\;M) &=
    \Let f = \pass{delay}(L) \In (\FST f)(\SND f, \pass{delay}(M))
    \qquad f \text{ fresh}
\end{align*}

\subsection{CC: Globalize (Clos4 \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} Clos5)}

Globalize hoists all code into a table of global definitions and replaces each piece of code by a reference to its entry.
\[
  \vec{d} = d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1}
  \qquad
  \pass{glob}(M, \vec{d}) = (M', \vec{d}')
\]
\begin{align*}
  \pass{glob}(x, \vec{d}) &= (x, \vec{d}) \\
  \pass{glob}(\fun{e\,y.\,N}, \vec{d}) &= (\funref{|\vec{d}_1|},\; \vec{d}_1, (\key{fun}\; e\,y.\,N'))
     \quad\text{where } (N', \vec{d}_1) = \pass{glob}(N, \vec{d}) \\
  \pass{glob}(\mathit{op}(\bar{x}_1.A_1, \ldots, \bar{x}_n.A_n), \vec{d}_0)
     &= (\mathit{op}(\bar{x}_1.A'_1, \ldots, \bar{x}_n.A'_n), \vec{d}_n)
     \quad\text{where } (A'_i, \vec{d}_i) = \pass{glob}(A_i, \vec{d}_{i-1}) \\[1ex]
  \pass{globalize}(M) &= (\vec{d};\; M') \quad\text{where } (M', \vec{d}) = \pass{glob}(M, \epsilon)
\end{align*}

\subsection{Remove Complex Operands (Clos5 \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} Monadic Clos5)}

Remove complex operands binds each complex operand to a fresh variable with a $\key{let}$, from left to right, so that every operand becomes an atom.
\[
  \vec{b} = x_1{=}M_1, \ldots, x_k{=}M_k
  \qquad
  \pass{atomize}(M_1, \ldots, M_n) = (\vec{b};\; a_1, \ldots, a_n)
\]
\begin{align*}
  \pass{atomize}(\epsilon) &= (\epsilon;\; \epsilon) \\
  \pass{atomize}(a, \vec{M}) &= (\vec{b};\; a, \vec{a})
     \quad\text{where } (\vec{b}; \vec{a}) = \pass{atomize}(\vec{M}) \\
  \pass{atomize}(M, \vec{M}) &= (x{=}M, \vec{b};\; x, \vec{a})
     \quad\text{where } (\vec{b}; \vec{a}) = \pass{atomize}(\vec{M}),\
     M \text{ not an atom},\ x \text{ fresh} \\[1ex]
  \pass{lets}(\epsilon, N) &= N \\
  \pass{lets}((x{=}M, \vec{b}), N) &= \Let x = M \In \pass{lets}(\vec{b}, N)
\end{align*}
\[
  \mathit{op}(M_1, \ldots, M_n) \in \{\, L(M, N),\ \pair{M}{N},\ \FST M,\ \SND M,\ \prim{p}{M}{N},\
     \tuple{M_1, \ldots, M_n},\ \prj{i} M,\ \inl M,\ \inr M \,\}
\]
\begin{align*}
  \pass{rco}(\mathit{op}(M_1, \ldots, M_n)) &= \pass{lets}(\vec{b}, \mathit{op}(a_1, \ldots, a_n)) \\
  &\qquad \text{where } (\vec{b}; a_1, \ldots, a_n) = \pass{atomize}(\pass{rco}(M_1), \ldots, \pass{rco}(M_n)) \\
  \pass{rco}(\Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N}) &= \pass{lets}(\vec{b}, \Case{a}{x}{M'}{y}{N'}) \\
  &\qquad \text{where } (\vec{b}; a) = \pass{atomize}(\pass{rco}(L)),\ M' = \pass{rco}(M),\ N' = \pass{rco}(N) \\
  \pass{rco}((d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)) &= (\pass{rco}(d_0), \ldots, \pass{rco}(d_{m-1});\; \pass{rco}(M)) \\
  \pass{rco}(\key{fun}\; e\,y.\,N) &= \key{fun}\; e\,y.\,\pass{rco}(N)
\end{align*}
An atom left in place is evaluated after the complex operands that follow
it. This preserves errors in the machines and interpreters, which
check that the operator of $L(M, N)$ is code only at the call, but an
unbound variable would error later: $\pair{x}{L}$, with $L$ a loop,
errors in the empty environment, while
$\Let y = L \In \pair{x}{y}$ diverges. So the operational proofs of rco
assume well-scoped programs.

\paragraph{Well-scoped programs.}
\begin{align*}
  \mathit{Scoped}_5((d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)) &\iff \FV(M) = \emptyset \land
    \forall k < m.\; \FV(d_k) = \emptyset \\
  \FV(\key{fun}\; e\,y.\,N) &= \FV(N) \setminus \{e, y\}
\end{align*}

\begin{lemma}[The Compiler Produces Well-Scoped Programs]\label{lem:compile-scoped}
For every ISWIM term $M$,
\[
  \FV(M) = \emptyset \implies \mathit{Scoped}_5(\pass{compile}(M)) .
\]
\end{lemma}

\subsection{Lift Locals (Monadic Clos5 \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} Imp)}

Lift locals gives every variable of a body a slot of the body's frame,
declared up front, and turns $\key{let}$ into assignment. A variable gets
the slot numbered by its binding depth: a $\key{let}$ or $\key{case}$ at
depth $d$ assigns slot $d$, and the code under it is at depth $d+1$.
A slot at depth $d$ is assigned only by code at depth $d$ or more, where
no variable in scope names it, so no assignment clobbers a variable in
scope. The map $\varphi : \X \rightharpoonup \mathbb{N}$ gives the slots
of the variables in scope, and $n$ is the size of the frame.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{lift}_{n,\varphi,d}(x) &= \varphi(x)
    \qquad (n \text{ if } x \notin \mathrm{dom}(\varphi)) \\
  \pass{lift}_{n,\varphi,d}(\Let x = M \In N) &=
    \assign{d}{\pass{lift}_{n,\varphi,d}(M)}{\pass{lift}_{n,\ext{\varphi}{x}{d},d+1}(N)} \\
  \pass{lift}_{n,\varphi,d}(\Case{a}{x}{M}{y}{N}) &=
    \key{case}\;\pass{lift}_{n,\varphi,d}(a)\;\key{of}_{d} \\
  &\qquad \key{inl} \Rightarrow \pass{lift}_{n,\ext{\varphi}{x}{d},d+1}(M)
    \mid \key{inr} \Rightarrow \pass{lift}_{n,\ext{\varphi}{y}{d},d+1}(N) \\[1ex]
  \mathit{height}(\Let x = M \In N) &= \max(\mathit{height}(M),\, 1 + \mathit{height}(N)) \\
  \mathit{height}(\Case{a}{x}{M}{y}{N}) &= 1 + \max(\mathit{height}(M), \mathit{height}(N)) \\
  \mathit{height}(M) &= 0 \qquad \text{otherwise} \\[1ex]
  \pass{lift}(\key{fun}\; e\,y.\,N) &= (n;\; \pass{lift}_{n,\{e \mapsto 0,\, y \mapsto 1\},2}(N))
    \quad\text{where } n = 2 + \mathit{height}(N) \\
  \pass{lift}((d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)) &=
    (\pass{lift}(d_0), \ldots, \pass{lift}(d_{m-1});\; b) \\
  &\qquad\text{where } b = (n;\; \pass{lift}_{n,\emptyset,0}(M)),\ n = \mathit{height}(M)
\end{align*}
A variable out of scope gets the slot $n$, beyond the frame, so that it
errors, as an unbound variable does in Clos5.

\subsection{Explicate Control (Imp \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} CFG)}

Explicate control flattens the nested assignments and cases of a body
into blocks, in continuation-passing style. $\pass{tail}(e)$ translates
$e$ in tail position, and $\pass{assign}(e, s, k)$ translates the
assignment of $e$ to slot $s$ followed by the tail $k$. Both add blocks
to the body's list; $\pass{add}(t)$ adds the block $t$ and returns its
label, the number of blocks before it. A case on the right of an
assignment adds $k$ as a block, the join point, which its branches jump
to, so $k$ is not duplicated.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{tail}(r) &= \key{return}\; r \\
  \pass{tail}(\assign{s}{e_1}{e_2}) &= \pass{assign}(e_1, s, \pass{tail}(e_2)) \\
  \pass{tail}(\Casei{a}{s}{e_1}{e_2}) &=
    \key{case}\;a\;\key{of}_{s}\;\key{goto}\;l_1 \mid \key{goto}\;l_2 \\
  &\qquad \text{where } l_1 = \pass{add}(\pass{tail}(e_1)),\ l_2 = \pass{add}(\pass{tail}(e_2)) \\[1ex]
  \pass{assign}(r, s, k) &= \assign{s}{r}{k} \\
  \pass{assign}(\assign{s'}{e_1}{e_2}, s, k) &= \pass{assign}(e_1, s', \pass{assign}(e_2, s, k)) \\
  \pass{assign}(\Casei{a}{s'}{e_1}{e_2}, s, k) &=
    \key{case}\;a\;\key{of}_{s'}\;\key{goto}\;l_1 \mid \key{goto}\;l_2 \\
  &\qquad \text{where } l = \pass{add}(k),\
    l_i = \pass{add}(\pass{assign}(e_i, s, \key{goto}\;l)) \\[1ex]
  \pass{explicate}((n;\; e)) &= (n;\; \vec{t};\; t)
    \quad\text{where } t = \pass{tail}(e) \text{ and } \vec{t} \text{ are the blocks it adds}
\end{align*}
and $\pass{explicate}$ maps over the definitions and main of a program.
Every block is added after the blocks it jumps to.

\subsection{Select Instructions (CFG \texorpdfstring{$\to$}{->} X86)}

Select instructions turns each statement into a few instructions; the
blocks keep their labels. $\pass{sel}(r, d)$ computes $r$ into the
location $d$, with $\bar{a}$ the operand for the atom $a$.
\begin{align*}
  \pass{sel}(a, d) &= \key{movq}\; \bar{a}, d \\
  \pass{sel}(a_1(a_2, a_3), d) &= \key{movq}\; \bar{a}_2, \%\key{rdi};\
    \key{movq}\; \bar{a}_3, \%\key{rsi};\ \key{callq}\; \bar{a}_1;\
    \key{movq}\; \%\key{rax}, d \\
  \pass{sel}(\key{sub}(a_1, a_2), d) &= \key{movq}\; \bar{a}_1, \%\key{rax};\
    \key{subq}\; \bar{a}_2, \%\key{rax};\ \key{movq}\; \%\key{rax}, d \\
  \pass{sel}(\key{eq}(a_1, a_2), d) &= \key{cmpq}\; \bar{a}_2, \bar{a}_1;\ \key{sete}\; d \\
  \pass{sel}(\pair{a_1}{a_2}, d) &= \key{alloc}\; \key{pair}\; \bar{a}_1, \bar{a}_2, d
    \qquad\text{and likewise for tuples and injections} \\
  \pass{sel}(\FST a, d) &= \key{load}\; \key{fst}\; \bar{a}, d
    \qquad\text{and likewise for } \SND,\ \prj{i} \\[1ex]
  \pass{sel}(\key{return}\; r) &= \pass{sel}(r, \%\key{rax});\ \key{retq} \\
  \pass{sel}(\assign{s}{r}{t}) &= \pass{sel}(r, s);\ \pass{sel}(t) \\
  \pass{sel}(\key{goto}\; l) &= \key{jmp}\; l \\
  \pass{sel}(\key{case}\;a\;\key{of}_{s}\;\key{goto}\;l_1 \mid \key{goto}\;l_2) &=
    \key{testinl}\; \bar{a};\ \key{load}\; \key{payload}\; \bar{a}, s;\ \key{je}\; l_1, l_2
\end{align*}
Subtraction goes through $\%\key{rax}$, so it is right also when $s$ is
$a_2$. The start of each global definition begins with the prologue
$\key{movq}\; \%\key{rdi}, 0;\ \key{movq}\; \%\key{rsi}, 1$, which moves its
free variables and argument to the slots where its body expects them.

%===============================================================================
\section{Denotational: Graph Model}\label{sec:u-den}

\subsection{Values and Operators}

\subsubsection{Values}

\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  c &\in& \text{constants} \qquad x, y, z, e, f \in \X \\[1ex]
  \V \ni d, u, v, w &::=& c \mid V \mapsto w \mid \nu \mid \omega
     \mid \pairv{u}{v}
     \mid \tup{i}{v} \mid \Left{v} \mid \Right{v} \mid \emptytup \\
  U, V, W &\in& \FSET{\V} \\
  D, E, X, Y &\in& \SET{\V} \\
  F, G &:& \SET{\V} \to \SET{\V}
\end{array}
\]

\[
\begin{array}{rcl}
  \rho &:& \X \to \SET{\V} \\
  \rho_0(x) &=& \{\omega\} \\
  D \simeq E &\iff& D \subseteq E \text{ and } E \subseteq D \\
  \rho \simeq \rho' &\iff& \forall x.\; \rho(x) \simeq \rho'(x)
\end{array}
\]

\subsubsection{Operators}\label{sec:den-ops}

\begin{align*}
  D \app E &= \{ w \mid \exists V \NE.\; (V \mapsto w) \in D,\; V \subseteq E \} \\
  \Lambda(F) &= \{ V \mapsto w \mid V \NE,\; V \approx V,\; w \in F(V) \} \cup \{ \nu \} \\
  \mathcal{B}(c) &= \{ c \} \\
  \key{pair}(D, E) &= \{ \pairv{u}{v} \mid u \in D,\; v \in E \} \\
  \key{car}(D) &= \{ u \mid \exists v.\; \pairv{u}{v} \in D \} \\
  \key{cdr}(D) &= \{ v \mid \exists u.\; \pairv{u}{v} \in D \} \\
  \mathcal{T}() &= \{ \emptytup \} \\
  \mathcal{T}(D_1,\ldots,D_n) &= \{ \tup{i}{d} \mid d \in D_i,\; \forall j.\; D_j \NE \}
     \qquad (n > 0) \\
  \key{proj}_i(D) &= \{ d \mid \tup{i}{d} \in D \} \\
  \key{inl}(D) &= \{ \Left{d} \mid d \in D \} \\
  \key{inr}(D) &= \{ \Right{d} \mid d \in D \} \\
  \mathcal{C}(D, F, G) &=
    \{ w \mid \exists V \NE.\; V \approx V,\; (\forall v \in V.\; \Left{v} \in D),\; w \in F(V) \} \\
    &\cup\; \{ w \mid \exists V \NE.\; V \approx V,\; (\forall v \in V.\; \Right{v} \in D),\; w \in G(V) \} \\
  \key{guard}(D_1,\ldots,D_n; E) &= \{ w \mid (\forall i.\; D_i \NE),\; w \in E \} \\
  \key{prim}_p(D, E) &= \{ \res{\delta(p, n, m)} \mid n \in D,\; m \in E \}
\end{align*}
where $\res{n} = n$, $\res{\mathsf{true}} = \Left{\emptytup}$, and $\res{\mathsf{false}} = \Right{\emptytup}$.
The binders $\Lambda$ and $\mathcal{C}$ bind only finite sets that are
consistent ($V \approx V$, Section~\ref{sec:consistency}), so a denotation
under a consistent environment has no inconsistent elements.

\begin{align*}
  D \odot E &= (\key{car}(D) \app \key{cdr}(D)) \app E
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{Consistency}\label{sec:consistency}

\begin{align*}
  c \sim c' &\iff c = c' \\
  (V \mapsto w) \sim (V' \mapsto w') &\iff (V \approx V' \implies w \sim w') \\
  (V \mapsto w) \sim \nu, \quad \nu \sim (V \mapsto w), \quad \nu \sim \nu &\iff \mathsf{true} \\
  \omega \sim \omega, \quad \emptytup \sim \emptytup &\iff \mathsf{true} \\
  \pairv{u}{v} \sim \pairv{u'}{v'} &\iff u \sim u' \land v \sim v' \\
  \tup{i}{d} \sim \tup{j}{d'} &\iff n = n' \land (i \neq j \lor d \sim d')
     \qquad (i \in \{1..n\},\; j \in \{1..n'\}) \\
  \Left{d} \sim \Left{d'} &\iff d \sim d' \\
  \Right{d} \sim \Right{d'} &\iff d \sim d' \\
  u \sim v &\iff \mathsf{false} \quad \text{otherwise} \\[1ex]
  D \approx E &\iff \forall u \in D, v \in E.\; u \sim v \\
  \consis(D) &\iff D \approx D \\
  \consis(\rho) &\iff \forall x.\; \consis(\rho(x))
\end{align*}
Every value is consistent with itself ($v \sim v$), so $\consis(\{v\})$.

\paragraph{Preservation.}
A relation $R$ between sets is preserved by the operators of a semantics
when each operator maps related arguments to related results, an
argument that binds a variable being a function that maps related sets
to related results.

\begin{lemma}[A Semantics Preserves Relations]\label{lem:pres}
Let $R$ be preserved by the operators of $\SEM{-}^{\ell}$, with
$\{\omega\} \mathrel{R} \{\omega\}$. If $\rho(x) \mathrel{R} \rho'(x)$ for
every $x$, then $\SEM{M}^{\ell}\rho \mathrel{R} \SEM{M}^{\ell}\rho'$.
\end{lemma}
With $R = {\subseteq}$, each semantics is monotone. With $R = {\approx}$,
which the operators preserve, each is consistent:
\begin{lemma}[Consistency]\label{lem:con}
If $\consis(\rho)$, then $\consis(\SEM{M}^{\ell}\rho)$ for
$\ell \in \{\mathrm{ISWIM}, 3, 4\}$, and $\consis(\SEM{M}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho)$
when $\consis(T(k))$ for every $k$. For all Clos5 definitions
$d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1}$ and every $k$,
\[
  \consis(\key{table}(d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1})(k)) .
\]
\end{lemma}

\subsubsection{Continuity}

\begin{align*}
  \rho' \subseteq \rho &\iff \forall x.\; \rho'(x) \subseteq \rho(x) \\
  \mathit{finiteNE}(\rho') &\iff \forall x.\; \exists V \NE.\; \rho'(x) = V \\
  \mathit{continuous}(\mathcal{E}) &\iff
    \forall \rho\, v.\; (\forall x.\; \rho(x) \NE) \land v \in \mathcal{E}(\rho) \\
    &\qquad \implies \exists \rho'.\; \mathit{finiteNE}(\rho'),\; \rho' \subseteq \rho,\; v \in \mathcal{E}(\rho')
\end{align*}

%===============================================================================
\subsection{Semantics}

\begin{align*}
  \SEM{-}^{\mathrm{ISWIM}}, \SEM{-}^{\mathrm{2}}, \ldots, \SEM{-}^{\mathrm{4}}
    &: \mathit{Term} \to (\X \to \SET{\V}) \to \SET{\V} \\
  \SEM{-}^{\mathrm{5}}_T
    &: \mathit{Term} \to (\X \to \SET{\V}) \to \SET{\V}
    \qquad (T : \mathbb{N} \to \SET{\V}) \\
  \SEM{-}^{\mathrm{5}} &: \mathit{Program} \to \SET{\V}
\end{align*}
For each language $\ell \in \{\mathrm{ISWIM}, 2, 3, 4\}$, $\SEM{-}^{\ell}$ is
defined by the common clauses below together with the clauses for $\ell$.

\subsubsection{Clauses common to all languages}

\begin{align*}
  \SEM{x}^{\ell}\rho &= \rho(x) \\
  \SEM{c}^{\ell}\rho &= \mathcal{B}(c) \\
  \SEM{\tuple{M_1,\ldots,M_n}}^{\ell}\rho &= \mathcal{T}(\SEM{M_1}^{\ell}\rho, \ldots, \SEM{M_n}^{\ell}\rho) \\
  \SEM{\prj{i} M}^{\ell}\rho &= \key{proj}_i(\SEM{M}^{\ell}\rho) \\
  \SEM{\inl M}^{\ell}\rho &= \key{inl}(\SEM{M}^{\ell}\rho) \\
  \SEM{\inr M}^{\ell}\rho &= \key{inr}(\SEM{M}^{\ell}\rho) \\
  \SEM{\Case{L}{x}{M}{y}{N}}^{\ell}\rho &=
     \mathcal{C}(\SEM{L}^{\ell}\rho,\; \lambda X.\, \SEM{M}^{\ell}\ext{\rho}{x}{X},\; \lambda Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\ell}\ext{\rho}{y}{Y}) \\
  \SEM{\prim{p}{L}{M}}^{\ell}\rho &= \key{prim}_p(\SEM{L}^{\ell}\rho, \SEM{M}^{\ell}\rho) \\
  \SEM{\Let x = M \In N}^{\ell}\rho &= \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \SEM{N}^{\ell}\ext{\rho}{x}{X}) \app \SEM{M}^{\ell}\rho
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{ISWIM}

\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\lam{x} N}^{\mathrm{ISWIM}}\rho &= \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \SEM{N}^{\mathrm{ISWIM}}\ext{\rho}{x}{X}) \\
  \SEM{L\;M}^{\mathrm{ISWIM}}\rho &= \SEM{L}^{\mathrm{ISWIM}}\rho \app \SEM{M}^{\mathrm{ISWIM}}\rho
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{Clos2}

\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\clos{z_1\ldots z_n}{x_1\ldots x_n\, y} N}^{\mathrm{2}}\rho &=
    \key{guard}(D_1, \ldots, D_n;\;
       \Lambda(\lambda Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\mathrm{2}}\rho_0(x_1{:=}D_1,\ldots,x_n{:=}D_n, y{:=}Y)))
    \\ &\qquad\text{where } D_i = \rho(z_i) \\
  \SEM{L\;M}^{\mathrm{2}}\rho &= \SEM{L}^{\mathrm{2}}\rho \app \SEM{M}^{\mathrm{2}}\rho
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{Clos3}

\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\clos{r_1\ldots r_n}{e\, y} N}^{\mathrm{3}}\rho &=
    \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \Lambda(\lambda Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\mathrm{3}}\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y)))
    \app \mathcal{T}(\SEM{r_1}^{\mathrm{3}}\rho, \ldots, \SEM{r_n}^{\mathrm{3}}\rho) \\
  \SEM{L\;M}^{\mathrm{3}}\rho &= \SEM{L}^{\mathrm{3}}\rho \app \SEM{M}^{\mathrm{3}}\rho
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{Clos4}

\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\fun{e\,y.\,N}}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho &= \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \Lambda(\lambda Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y))) \\
  \SEM{L(M, N)}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho &= (\SEM{L}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho \app \SEM{M}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho) \app \SEM{N}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho \\
  \SEM{\pair{M}{N}}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho &= \key{pair}(\SEM{M}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho, \SEM{N}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho) \\
  \SEM{\FST M}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho &= \key{car}(\SEM{M}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho) \\
  \SEM{\SND M}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho &= \key{cdr}(\SEM{M}^{\mathrm{4}}\rho)
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{Clos5}

\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\funref{k}}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho &= T(k) \\
  \SEM{L(M, N)}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho &= (\SEM{L}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho \app \SEM{M}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho) \app \SEM{N}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho \\
  \SEM{\pair{M}{N}}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho &= \key{pair}(\SEM{M}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho, \SEM{N}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho) \\
  \SEM{\FST M}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho &= \key{car}(\SEM{M}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho) \\
  \SEM{\SND M}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho &= \key{cdr}(\SEM{M}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho)
\end{align*}
together with the common clauses, with $\SEM{-}^{\mathrm{5}}_T$ in place of $\SEM{-}^{\ell}$.

\begin{align*}
  \key{code}_T(\key{fun}\; e\,y.\,N) &= \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \Lambda(\lambda Y.\, \SEM{N}^{\mathrm{5}}_T\rho_0(e{:=}X, y{:=}Y))) \\[1ex]
  \key{table}(d_0,\ldots,d_{m-1})(k) &=
    \begin{cases}
      \key{code}_{\key{table}(d_0,\ldots,d_{k-1})}(d_k) & k < m \\
      \emptyset & \text{otherwise}
    \end{cases} \\[1ex]
  \SEM{(d_0, \ldots, d_{m-1};\; M)}^{\mathrm{5}} &= \SEM{M}^{\mathrm{5}}_{\key{table}(d_0,\ldots,d_{m-1})}\,\rho_0
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{Imp and CFG: what they share}

A store $\sigma$ gives each slot a meaning. An expression or tail runs in
a frame of $n$ slots. Reading a slot beyond the frame gives $\{\omega\}$,
as an unbound variable does in Clos5; assigning one errors, so the
construct means $\emptyset$ ($\mathit{checked}_n$). A simple expression
leaves the store as it was. With the table $T$ and $n$ fixed:
\begin{align*}
  \sigma &: \mathbb{N} \to \SET{\V} \\
  \mathit{read}_n(\sigma, s) &=
    \begin{cases} \sigma(s) & s < n \\ \{\omega\} & \text{otherwise} \end{cases} \qquad
  \mathit{checked}_n(s, D) =
    \begin{cases} D & s < n \\ \emptyset & \text{otherwise} \end{cases} \\[1ex]
  \mathcal{A}_\sigma(s) = \mathit{read}_n(\sigma, s), \quad
  \mathcal{A}_\sigma(c) &= \mathcal{B}(c), \quad
  \mathcal{A}_\sigma(\funref{k}) = T(k) \\[1ex]
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(a) &= \mathcal{A}_\sigma(a) \\
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(a_1(a_2, a_3)) &= (\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_1) \app \mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_2)) \app \mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_3) \\
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\pair{a_1}{a_2}) &= \key{pair}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_1), \mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_2)) \\
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\FST a) &= \key{car}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a)) \qquad
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\SND a) = \key{cdr}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a)) \\
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\tuple{a_1, \ldots, a_l}) &= \mathcal{T}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_1), \ldots, \mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_l)) \qquad
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\prj{i} a) = \key{proj}_i(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a)) \\
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\inl a) &= \key{inl}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a)) \qquad
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\inr a) = \key{inr}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a)) \\
  \mathcal{S}_\sigma(\prim{p}{a_1}{a_2}) &= \key{prim}_p(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_1), \mathcal{A}_\sigma(a_2))
\end{align*}
A function body runs in a fresh frame, with the free variables in slot
$0$ and the argument in slot $1$; main runs in a frame of zeros. Given
the code of a body, the table of a list of bodies gives each the table
of the older ones:
\begin{align*}
  \key{zeros}(t) &= \{0\} \\
  \key{frame}(X, Y)(0) = X, \quad \key{frame}(X, Y)(1) &= Y, \quad
  \key{frame}(X, Y)(t) = \{0\} \quad (t \geq 2) \\[1ex]
  \key{table}(b_0,\ldots,b_{m-1})(k) &=
    \begin{cases}
      \key{code}_{\key{table}(b_0,\ldots,b_{k-1})}(b_k) & k < m \\
      \emptyset & \text{otherwise}
    \end{cases}
\end{align*}

\subsubsection{Imp, continuation-passing}

The semantics the main proof uses. An expression runs in a frame of $n$
slots, given the store $\sigma$ as it enters and a continuation $\kappa$,
the meaning of what runs after it: $\kappa(D, \sigma')$ is the meaning of
the rest of the body, given the value $D$ of the expression and the
store $\sigma'$ it leaves. So each run of an expression passes its own
store on.
\begin{align*}
  \kappa \in \mathit{Kont} &= \SET{\V} \to (\mathbb{N} \to \SET{\V}) \to \SET{\V} \\
  \SEM{-}^{\mathrm{ImpK}}_{T,n} &: \mathit{Exp} \to (\mathbb{N} \to \SET{\V}) \to \mathit{Kont} \to \SET{\V}
    \qquad (T : \mathbb{N} \to \SET{\V}) \\
  \SEM{-}^{\mathrm{ImpK}} &: \mathit{Program} \to \SET{\V}
\end{align*}
With $T$ and $n$ fixed, writing $\SEM{e}\sigma\,\kappa$ for $\SEM{e}^{\mathrm{ImpK}}_{T,n}\sigma\,\kappa$:
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{r}\sigma\,\kappa &= \kappa(\mathcal{S}_\sigma(r), \sigma) \\
  \SEM{\Casei{a}{s}{e_1}{e_2}}\sigma\,\kappa &= \mathit{checked}_n(s,
    \mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a),\; \lambda X.\, \SEM{e_1}\ext{\sigma}{s}{X}\,\kappa,\;
      \lambda X.\, \SEM{e_2}\ext{\sigma}{s}{X}\,\kappa)) \\
  \SEM{\assign{s}{e_1}{e_2}}\sigma\,\kappa &= \SEM{e_1}\sigma\,(\lambda D\, \sigma'.\,
    \mathit{checked}_n(s, \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \SEM{e_2}\ext{\sigma'}{s}{X}\,\kappa) \app D)) \\[1ex]
  \key{halt}(D, \sigma) &= D \\
  \key{code}^{\mathrm{K}}_T((n;\; e)) &= \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \Lambda(\lambda Y.\,
    \SEM{e}^{\mathrm{ImpK}}_{T,n}\,\key{frame}(X, Y)\,\key{halt})) \\
  \SEM{(b_0, \ldots, b_{m-1};\; (n;\; e))}^{\mathrm{ImpK}} &=
    \SEM{e}^{\mathrm{ImpK}}_{\key{table}^{\mathrm{K}}(b_0,\ldots,b_{m-1}),n}\,\key{zeros}\,\key{halt}
\end{align*}
where $\key{table}^{\mathrm{K}}$ is $\key{table}$ with $\key{code}^{\mathrm{K}}$.

\subsubsection{Imp, direct}

An alternative, kept alongside: given the store as it enters, an
expression means a set of values and the store as it leaves.
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{-}^{\mathrm{ImpD}}_{T,n} &: \mathit{Exp} \to (\mathbb{N} \to \SET{\V}) \to \SET{\V} \times (\mathbb{N} \to \SET{\V}) \\
  \SEM{-}^{\mathrm{ImpD}} &: \mathit{Program} \to \SET{\V}
\end{align*}
\begin{align*}
  \mathit{checked}^{\mathrm{D}}_n(s, \sigma, r) &=
    \begin{cases} r & s < n \\ (\emptyset, \sigma) & \text{otherwise} \end{cases} \\[1ex]
  r{\downarrow}t &=
    \begin{cases} \pi_2(r)(t) & \pi_1(r) \NE \\ \emptyset & \text{otherwise} \end{cases} \\
  \key{let}_s(D, E) &= (\Lambda(\lambda X.\, \pi_1(E(X))) \app D,\;
    \lambda t.\, \Lambda(\lambda X.\, E(X){\downarrow}t) \app D) \\
  \key{case}_s(A, E_1, E_2) &= (\mathcal{C}(A,\; \lambda X.\, \pi_1(E_1(X)),\; \lambda X.\, \pi_1(E_2(X))), \\
    &\qquad \lambda t.\, \mathcal{C}(A,\; \lambda X.\, E_1(X){\downarrow}t,\; \lambda X.\, E_2(X){\downarrow}t))
\end{align*}
where $\pi_1$ and $\pi_2$ take the value and the store of a pair. The
slot $t$ of the store a let or case leaves collects slot $t$ of the runs
of its body that have a value ($r{\downarrow}t$): a run that errors
or diverges leaves no store.
With $T$ and $n$ fixed, writing $\SEM{e}\sigma$ for $\SEM{e}^{\mathrm{ImpD}}_{T,n}\sigma$:
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{r}\sigma &= (\mathcal{S}_\sigma(r), \sigma) \\
  \SEM{\Casei{a}{s}{e_1}{e_2}}\sigma &= \mathit{checked}^{\mathrm{D}}_n(s, \sigma,
    \key{case}_s(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a),\; \lambda X.\, \SEM{e_1}\ext{\sigma}{s}{X},\; \lambda X.\, \SEM{e_2}\ext{\sigma}{s}{X})) \\
  \SEM{\assign{s}{e_1}{e_2}}\sigma &= \mathit{checked}^{\mathrm{D}}_n(s, \sigma,
    \key{let}_s(D_1,\; \lambda X.\, \SEM{e_2}\ext{\sigma_1}{s}{X}))
    \quad\text{where } (D_1, \sigma_1) = \SEM{e_1}\sigma \\[1ex]
  \key{code}^{\mathrm{D}}_T((n;\; e)) &= \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \Lambda(\lambda Y.\, \pi_1(\SEM{e}^{\mathrm{ImpD}}_{T,n}\key{frame}(X, Y)))) \\
  \SEM{(b_0, \ldots, b_{m-1};\; (n;\; e))}^{\mathrm{ImpD}} &=
    \pi_1(\SEM{e}^{\mathrm{ImpD}}_{\key{table}^{\mathrm{D}}(b_0,\ldots,b_{m-1}),n}\,\key{zeros})
\end{align*}
where $\key{table}^{\mathrm{D}}$ is $\key{table}$ with $\key{code}^{\mathrm{D}}$.

\subsubsection{CFG}

A tail runs in a frame of $n$ slots, given the meanings $B$ of the blocks
it can see, and means a set of values. The blocks of a body are given
meaning as the global definitions are: each with the meanings of the
blocks older than it, which are all it can jump to, so no fixed point is
needed. A jump to a label that is not among them means $\emptyset$.
\begin{align*}
  B &: \mathbb{N} \to (\mathbb{N} \to \SET{\V}) \to \SET{\V} \\
  \SEM{-}^{\mathrm{CFG}}_{T,n,B} &: \mathit{Tail} \to (\mathbb{N} \to \SET{\V}) \to \SET{\V} \\
  \SEM{-}^{\mathrm{CFG}} &: \mathit{Program} \to \SET{\V}
\end{align*}
With $T$, $n$, and $B$ fixed, writing $\SEM{t}\sigma$ for $\SEM{t}^{\mathrm{CFG}}_{T,n,B}\sigma$:
\begin{align*}
  \SEM{\key{return}\; r}\sigma &= \mathcal{S}_\sigma(r) \\
  \SEM{\assign{s}{r}{t}}\sigma &= \mathit{checked}_n(s,
    \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \SEM{t}\ext{\sigma}{s}{X}) \app \mathcal{S}_\sigma(r)) \\
  \SEM{\key{goto}\; l}\sigma &= B(l)(\sigma) \\
  \SEM{\key{case}\;a\;\key{of}_{s}\;\key{goto}\;l_1 \mid \key{goto}\;l_2}\sigma &=
    \mathit{checked}_n(s, \mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A}_\sigma(a),\;
      \lambda X.\, B(l_1)(\ext{\sigma}{s}{X}),\; \lambda X.\, B(l_2)(\ext{\sigma}{s}{X}))) \\[1ex]
  \key{blocks}_{T,n}(t_0, \ldots, t_{j-1})(l) &=
    \begin{cases}
      \SEM{t_l}^{\mathrm{CFG}}_{T,n,\key{blocks}_{T,n}(t_0, \ldots, t_{l-1})} & l < j \\
      \lambda \sigma.\, \emptyset & \text{otherwise}
    \end{cases} \\
  \mathit{run}_T((n;\; \vec{t};\; t)) &= \SEM{t}^{\mathrm{CFG}}_{T,n,\key{blocks}_{T,n}(\vec{t})} \\[1ex]
  \key{code}^{\mathrm{CFG}}_T(b) &= \Lambda(\lambda X.\, \Lambda(\lambda Y.\, \mathit{run}_T(b)(\key{frame}(X, Y)))) \\
  \SEM{(b_0, \ldots, b_{m-1};\; b)}^{\mathrm{CFG}} &=
    \mathit{run}_{\key{table}^{\mathrm{CFG}}(b_0,\ldots,b_{m-1})}(b)(\key{zeros})
\end{align*}
where $\key{table}^{\mathrm{CFG}}$ is $\key{table}$ with $\key{code}^{\mathrm{CFG}}$.

\subsubsection{X86}\label{sec:den-x86}

Continuation-passing, as Imp. The state of a body is its frame $\sigma$,
a store as in CFG; its registers $\rho$; and the flag $\varphi$, a set of
values: $\Left{\emptytup}$ is set, $\Right{\emptytup}$ is not set, and
$\emptyset$ is no flag. What runs from a point of a body on, $\kappa$,
means a set of values given the state. Every write is strict: a slot is
bound as in CFG, and a register or the flag holds a set $D$ only when
$D \NE$, so an instruction whose value is $\emptyset$ stops the body.
Reading an unset register or a clobbered flag gives $\emptyset$.
\begin{align*}
  \rho &: \{\rax, \rdi, \rsi\} \to \SET{\V} \qquad \varphi \in \SET{\V} \\
